c_{i,n}^{*}=\tfrac{1}{2}(b_{i,n}^{*}+R(b_{i,n}))=R(c_{i,n})=S^{-1}(c_{i,n}),

the operator
\Theta^{l}(c_{i,n})
is star preserving by Corollary
4.10
.

We now show that
(c_{i,n})_{(i,n)\in I\times\mathbb{N}}
converges to
\mathbbm{1}
weak
∗
in
\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
. We will show this first for
(b_{i,n})_{(i,n)\in I\times\mathbb{N}}
. Choose
\rho\in\ell^{1}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}),\omega\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
and set
y=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\rho)\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}}
. We have

(6.3)

\begin{split}&\quad\;\langle\Theta^{l}(b_{i,n})(y),\omega\rangle=\langle(\textnormal{id}\otimes\rho)(\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}}),\Theta^{l}(b_{i,n})_{*}(\omega)\rangle=\langle b_{i,n}(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}}),\rho\rangle\\
&=m_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl(t\mapsto\langle\tau_{t}(a_{i,n})(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}}),\rho\rangle\bigr)=m_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl(t\mapsto\langle\Theta^{l}(\tau_{t}(a_{i,n}))y,\omega\rangle\bigr).\end{split}

By continuity, the above equation holds for each
y\in\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
.

Let
\mathsf{H}
be a separable Hilbert space, and consider
x\in\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\odot\mathcal{K}(\mathsf{H})
and
\rho\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\odot\B(\mathsf{H})_{*}
. Then using linearity and (
6.3
), it follows that

\langle b_{i,n},\Omega_{x,\rho}\rangle=\langle(\Theta^{l}(b_{i,n})\otimes\textnormal{id})x,\rho\rangle=m_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl(t\mapsto\langle(\Theta^{l}(\tau_{t}(a_{i,n}))\otimes\textnormal{id})x,\rho\rangle\bigr).

Lemmas
4.11
and
6.7
imply

\langle b_{i,n},\Omega_{x,\rho}\rangle=m_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl(t\mapsto\langle(\Theta^{l}(a_{i,n})\otimes\textnormal{id})(\hat{\tau}_{-t}\otimes\textnormal{id})(x),\rho\circ(\hat{\tau}_{t}\otimes\textnormal{id})\rangle\bigr)=\langle a_{i,n},\Omega^{\tau}_{x,\rho}\rangle.

Both sides of the above equation are continuous with respect to
x,\rho
, hence it holds also for
x\in\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\otimes\mathcal{K}(\mathsf{H})
and
\rho\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\widehat{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H})_{*}
. Since
\Omega^{\tau}_{x,\rho}
is a normal functional, it follows that

\displaystyle\lim_{(i,n)\in I\times\mathbb{N}}\langle b_{i,n},\Omega_{x,\rho}\rangle

\displaystyle=\lim_{(i,n)\in I\times\mathbb{N}}\langle a_{i,n},\Omega^{\tau}_{x,\rho}\rangle=\langle\mathbbm{1},\Omega^{\tau}_{x,\rho}\rangle

\displaystyle=m_{\mathbb{R}}\big(t\mapsto\langle(\hat{\tau}_{-t}\otimes\textnormal{id})(x),\rho\circ(\hat{\tau}_{t}\otimes\textnormal{id})\rangle\big)=\langle x,\rho\rangle=\langle\mathbbm{1},\Omega_{x,\rho}\rangle

and so
b_{i,n}\xrightarrow[(i,n)\in I\times\mathbb{N}]{w^{*}}\mathbbm{1}
, as the functionals
\Omega_{x,\rho}
give all of
Q^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
, by Proposition
3.9
. Then using Proposition
4.9
,

\begin{split}&\quad\;\langle R(b_{i,n})^{*},\Omega_{x,\rho}\rangle=\langle S(b_{i,n}^{*}),\Omega_{x,\rho}\rangle=\langle(\Theta^{l}(b_{i,n})^{\dagger}\otimes\textnormal{id})x,\rho\rangle\\
&=\langle(\Theta^{l}(b_{i,n})\otimes\textnormal{id})(x^{*})^{*},\rho\rangle=\overline{\langle(\Theta^{l}(b_{i,n})\otimes\textnormal{id})(x^{*}),\overline{\rho}\rangle}\end{split}

which converges to
\overline{\langle x^{*},\overline{\rho}\rangle}=\langle x,\rho\rangle=\langle\mathbbm{1},\Omega_{x,\rho}\rangle
. We can conclude that
c_{i,n}=\tfrac{1}{2}(b_{i,n}+R(b_{i,n})^{*})
also converges weak
∗
to
\mathbbm{1}
.

We have now shown all the properties required of the net
(c_{i,n})_{(i,n)\in I\times\mathbb{N}}
except that each
\Theta^{l}(c_{i,n})
is unit preserving. By Lemma
3.2
, we know that there is a family of scalars
(\alpha_{i,n})_{(i,n)\in I\times\mathbb{N}}
with
\Theta^{l}(c_{i,n})(\mathbbm{1})=\alpha_{i,n}\mathbbm{1}
for each
(i,n)
. As
\Theta^{l}(c_{i,n})
is star-preserving, each
\alpha_{i,n}\in\mathbb{R}
. As
c_{i,n}\xrightarrow[(i,n)\in I\times\mathbb{N}]{}\mathbbm{1}
weak
∗
,
\Theta^{l}(c_{i,n})(\mathbbm{1})=\alpha_{i,n}\mathbbm{1}\xrightarrow[(i,n)\in I\times\mathbb{N}]{}\mathbbm{1}
weak
∗
and so
\alpha_{i,n}\xrightarrow[(i,n)\in I\times\mathbb{N}]{}1
. We may hence replace
c_{i,n}
by
\alpha_{i,n}^{-1}c_{i,n}
.

Finally, when
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
has central AP, then we can skip the first step (as
a_{i}\in\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})\cap\mathcal{Z}(\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
by assumption), and proceed as above to form
b_{i}
. It follows from (
6.1
) that
\hat{\tau}_{t}(U^{\alpha}_{i,i})=U^{\alpha}_{i,i}
, and the equality
(\hat{\tau}_{t}\otimes\tau_{t})\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}}=\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}}
, together with (
6.2
), shows
\tau_{t}(a_{i})=a_{i}
for each
t,i
. Thus actually
b_{i}=a_{i}
, and the final step of forming
c_{i}
, and rescaling, will also give central elements.
∎

In the unimodular case there is no difference between AP and central AP.

Proposition 6.8
.

Let
\mathbb{G}
be a unimodular discrete quantum group. Then
\mathbb{G}
has AP if and only if it has central AP.

Proof.

Since the Haar integral
h\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
is a trace there exists a unique state-preserving normal faithful conditional
expectation
E\colon\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow\widehat{\Delta}(\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}))\subseteq\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
.
Explicitly, we have

E(U^{\alpha}_{i,j}\otimes U^{\beta}_{k,l})=\frac{\delta_{\alpha\beta}\delta_{jk}}{\dim(\alpha)}\widehat{\Delta}(U^{\alpha}_{i,l}),

compare
[
9
, Section 6.3.2]
.
Set
\widehat{\Delta}^{\sharp}=\widehat{\Delta}^{-1}E:\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
. Given
a\in\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbb{G}))
define

\Psi(a)=\widehat{\Delta}^{\sharp}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Theta^{l}(a))\widehat{\Delta}\in\CB^{\sigma}(\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})).

That
\Psi(a)
is normal and completely bounded is clear, and note that we have
\|\Psi(a)\|_{cb}\leq\|a\|_{cb}
. Moreover, if
a
is finitely supported then
\Psi(a)
has finite-rank, see Remark
6.4
.

Define also
A:\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
by

A(f)=\sum_{\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})}\frac{\Tr_{\alpha}(f)}{\dim(\alpha)}p_{\alpha},

where
p_{\alpha}\in\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
is the central projection corresponding to
\B(\mathsf{H}_{\alpha})\subseteq\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
and
\Tr_{\alpha}\in\ell^{1}(\mathbb{G})
is the projection onto
\B(\mathsf{H}_{\alpha})
composed with the (non-normalised) trace on
\B(\mathsf{H}_{\alpha})
. Then
A
is a contractive linear map. Given
a\in\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbb{G}))
and
\omega\in h(\Pol(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\cdot)
, we compute

\begin{split}&\quad\;\lambda_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(\Psi(a)_{*}(\omega))=\sum_{\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})}\sum_{i,j=1}^{\dim(\alpha)}\langle\Psi(a)(U^{\alpha}_{i,j}),\omega\rangle e^{\alpha}_{i,j}\\
&=\sum_{\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})}\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{\dim(\alpha)}\langle\widehat{\Delta}^{\sharp}(U^{\alpha}_{i,k}\otimes\Theta^{l}(a)(U^{\alpha}_{k,j})),\omega\rangle e^{\alpha}_{i,j}=\sum_{\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})}\sum_{i,j,k,l=1}^{\dim(\alpha)}a^{\alpha}_{k,l}\langle\widehat{\Delta}^{\sharp}(U^{\alpha}_{i,k}\otimes U^{\alpha}_{l,j}),\omega\rangle e^{\alpha}_{i,j}\\
&=\sum_{\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})}\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{\dim(\alpha)}\tfrac{a^{\alpha}_{k,k}}{\dim(\alpha)}\langle U^{\alpha}_{i,j},\omega\rangle e^{\alpha}_{i,j}=\sum_{\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})}\sum_{i,j=1}^{\dim(\alpha)}\langle U^{\alpha}_{i,j},\omega\rangle\tfrac{\Tr_{\alpha}(a)}{\dim(\alpha)}e^{\alpha}_{i,j}=A(a)\lambda_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(\omega).\end{split}

Here we used Lemma
6.1
to compute the action of
\Theta^{l}(a)
, and notice that by the choice of
\omega
, all the sums involved are finite. As such
\omega
are dense in
\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
, it follows that
A(a)
is a left CB multiplier and
\Theta^{l}(A(a))=\Psi(a)
. In particular,
A(a)\in\mathcal{Z}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbb{G}))
and
\|A(a)\|_{cb}\leq\|a\|_{cb}
.

Now assume that
\mathbb{G}
has AP. Using Proposition
6.5
we find a net let
(f_{i})_{i\in I}
in
\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbb{G})
which converges weak
∗
to
\mathbbm{1}
in
\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbb{G}))
. In order to prove that
\mathbb{G}
has central AP, we shall show that
A(f_{i})\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\mathbbm{1}
weak
∗
in
\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbb{G}))
.
Take
x\in\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\otimes\mathcal{K}(\mathsf{H}),\omega\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\widehat{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H})_{*}
for a separable Hilbert space
\mathsf{H}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\langle A(f_{i}),\Omega_{x,\omega}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle(\Psi(f_{i})\otimes\textnormal{id})x,\omega\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle(\widehat{\Delta}^{\sharp}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Theta^{l}(f_{i})\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\Delta}\otimes\textnormal{id})x,\omega\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Theta^{l}(f_{i})\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\Delta}\otimes\textnormal{id})x,\omega\circ(\widehat{\Delta}^{\sharp}\otimes\textnormal{id})\rangle.

By applying Lemma
6.6
, with
\M=\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold{\Gamma}}),\N=\mathbb{C}
, it follows that

\lim_{i\in I}\langle A(f_{i}),\Omega_{x,\omega}\rangle=\langle(\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\Delta}\otimes\textnormal{id})x,\omega\circ(\widehat{\Delta}^{\sharp}\otimes\textnormal{id})\rangle=\langle x,\omega\rangle=\langle\mathbbm{1},\Omega_{x,\omega}\rangle,

hence showing that
A(f_{i})\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\mathbbm{1}
weak
∗
, as required.
∎

In the remainder of this section we relate AP to approximation properties of associated operator algebras. Let us start by recalling the appropriate von Neumann algebraic approximation property, see
[
32
, Section 2]
.

Definition 6.9
.

Let
\M
be a von Neumann algebra. Then
\M
has the
weak
∗
operator approximation property (W
∗
OAP)
if there exists a net
(\Theta_{i})_{i\in I}
of finite rank normal CB maps on
\M
which converges to the identity in the stable point-weak
∗
-topology, i.e.
(\Theta_{i}\otimes\textnormal{id})x\xrightarrow[i\in I]{w^{*}}x
for all separable Hilbert spaces
\mathsf{H}
and
x\in\M\bar{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H})
.

The following result was established by Kraus and Ruan for Kac algebras,
[
42
, Theorem 4.15]
, using the formally stronger definition of AP (which by Theorem
4.4

is equivalent to the definition taken in this paper). A result of this type was also obtained in
[
13
, Proposition 4.7]
, again with a formally stronger definition of AP, and under the assumption that
\mathbb{G}
is strongly inner amenable. We record a proof for the convenience of a reader.

Proposition 6.10
.

Let
\mathbb{G}
be a locally compact quantum group. If
\mathbb{G}
has AP, then
\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
has W
∗
OAP.

Proof.

Assume that
\mathbb{G}
has AP. By Theorem
4.4
there is a net
(\widehat{\lambda}(\omega_{i}))_{i\in I}
in
\A(\mathbb{G})
such that
\Theta^{l}(\widehat{\lambda}(\omega_{i}))\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\textnormal{id}
in the stable point-weak
∗
-topology of
\CB^{\sigma}(\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}))
. Extend
\omega_{i}\in\operatorname{L}^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
to
\tilde{\omega}_{i}\in\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))_{*}
with the same norm and define

\begin{split}\Psi_{i}\colon\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))\ni T\mapsto&(\tilde{\omega}_{i}\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl(\mathrm{V}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(T\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathrm{V}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}*}\bigr)\in\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})).\end{split}

Clearly
\Psi_{i}
is a normal CB map on
\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
, and as
\mathrm{V}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})^{\prime}\bar{\otimes}\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
the image of
\Psi_{i}
lies in
\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
. Note that if
x\in\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
then

\begin{split}\Psi_{i}(x)=(\tilde{\omega}_{i}\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl(\mathrm{V}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(x\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathrm{V}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}*}\bigr)=(\tilde{\omega}_{i}\otimes\textnormal{id})\widehat{\Delta}(x)=\Theta^{l}(\widehat{\lambda}(\omega_{i}))(x).\end{split}

Thus
\Psi_{i}
is an extension of
\Theta^{l}(\widehat{\lambda}(\omega_{i}))
to all of
\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
.
As
\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
has W
∗
CPAP, see
[
10
, Propositions 2.1.4, 2.2.7]
, there is a net
(\Upsilon_{\lambda})_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
of finite rank normal unital CP maps on
\B(\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G}))
which converges to the identity in the point-weak
∗
-topology. Consider now the maps

\Psi_{i,\lambda}=\Psi_{i}\circ\Upsilon_{\lambda}|_{\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})}\colon\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\rightarrow\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}).

These are normal and CB, and, because
\Upsilon_{\lambda}
has finite rank, also each
\Psi_{i,\lambda}
is finite-rank. Fix a separable Hilbert space
\mathsf{H}
and finite sets
F\subseteq\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\bar{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H}),\;G\subseteq\operatorname{L}^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\widehat{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H})_{*}
and
0<\varepsilon<1
. Given
x\in F,\rho\in G
, we have

\langle(\Psi_{i}\otimes\textnormal{id})x,\rho\rangle=\langle(\Theta^{l}(\widehat{\lambda}(\omega_{i}))\otimes\textnormal{id})\,x,\rho\rangle\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\langle x,\rho\rangle.

Thus there is
i(F,G,\varepsilon)\in I
such that

|\langle(\Psi_{i(F,G,\varepsilon)}\otimes\textnormal{id})x-x,\rho\rangle|\leq\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}\qquad(x\in F,\rho\in G).

Next, since
\Psi_{i(F,G,\varepsilon)}
is normal, we have

|\langle(\Psi_{i(F,G,\varepsilon)}\circ\Upsilon_{\lambda}\otimes\textnormal{id})x-(\Psi_{i(F,G,\varepsilon)}\otimes\textnormal{id})x,\rho\rangle|\xrightarrow[\lambda\in\Lambda]{}0\qquad(x\in F,\rho\in G),

hence there is
\lambda(F,G,\varepsilon)\in\Lambda
so that

|\langle(\Psi_{i(F,G,\varepsilon)}\circ\Upsilon_{\lambda(F,G,\varepsilon)}\otimes\textnormal{id})x-(\Psi_{i(F,G,\varepsilon)}\otimes\textnormal{id})x,\rho\rangle|\leq\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}\qquad(x\in F,\rho\in G),

and by triangle inequality

|\langle(\Psi_{i(F,G,\varepsilon)}\circ\Upsilon_{\lambda(F,G,\varepsilon)}\otimes\textnormal{id})x-x,\rho\rangle|\leq\varepsilon\qquad(x\in F,\rho\in G).

Consequently, the net
(\Psi_{i(F,G,\varepsilon),\lambda(F,G,\varepsilon)})_{(F,G,\varepsilon)}
(indexed by finite subsets of
\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\bar{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H})
,

\operatorname{L}^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\widehat{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H})_{*}
and
\left]0,1\right[
) shows that
\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
has the W
∗
OAP.
∎

Remark 6.11
.

An analogous argument shows that if
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
is coamenable then
\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
has W
∗
CPAP. In fact, a formally stronger result holds: W
∗
CPAP of
\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
follows from amenability of
\mathbb{G}
by
[
7
, Theorem 3.3]
.

When the quantum group
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
is discrete and has AP, we obtain approximation properties also for the associated
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra. If
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
is furthermore unimodular, the converse implications hold. These results are already known (see e.g.
[
42
, Theorem 5.13]
), hence we skip the proof.
For the definition of OAP and strong OAP, see
[
23
, Page 204]
or
[
10
, Section 12.4]
, for example.

Proposition 6.12
.

Let
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
be a discrete quantum group. Consider the following conditions:

(1)

\mathbbold{\Gamma}
has AP,

(2)

\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
has strong OAP,

(3)

\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
has OAP,

(4)

\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
has W
∗
OAP.

Then
(1)\Rightarrow(2)\Rightarrow(3)
and
(1)\Rightarrow(4)
. If
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
is unimodular then all the above conditions are equivalent.

Remark 6.13
.

Combining Proposition
6.12
and Proposition
6.8
, we see that when
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
is unimodular and
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
has W
∗
OAP, then
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
has the central AP.

Following
[
68
, Definition 1.27]
, we say that a discrete quantum group
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
is exact when the
reduced crossed product functor

\mathbbold{\Gamma}\ltimes_{r}-
,
preserves short exact sequences.

Corollary 6.14
.

Let
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
be a discrete quantum group. If
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
has AP then it is exact.

Proof.

By Proposition
6.12
, the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
has strong OAP. It is then exact by a combination of
[
23
, Corollary 11.3.2]
and
[
37
, Theorem 1.1]
. The result now follows from
[
68
, Proposition 1.28]
.
∎

We end this section with a result which shows that for a discrete quantum group
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
, AP is equivalent to a strengthening of W
∗
OAP of
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold{\Gamma}})
which takes into
consideration
\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
. Let us introduce this strengthening in a general setting, compare
[
40
, Definition 6.9]
.

Definition 6.15
.

Let
(\M,\theta)
be a von Neumann algebra with a n.s.f. weight.

•

Let
\Phi\in\CB^{\sigma}(\M)
be a normal CB map satisfying
\Phi(\mathfrak{N}_{\theta})\subseteq\mathfrak{N}_{\theta}
. We say that
\Phi
has an
\LL^{2}
-implementation if there is
T\in\B(\mathsf{H}_{\theta})
such that
\Lambda_{\theta}(\Phi(x))=T\Lambda_{\theta}(x)
for
x\in\mathfrak{N}_{\theta}
.

•

Let
\N\subseteq\B(\mathsf{H}_{\theta})
be a von Neumann algebra. We say that
(\M,\theta)
has W
∗
OAP relative to
\N
if there is a net
(\Phi_{i})_{i\in I}
such that:

–

each
\Phi_{i}
is a normal, CB, finite rank map on
\M
,

–

each
\Phi_{i}
satisfies

\Phi_{i}(\mathfrak{N}_{\theta})\subseteq\mathfrak{N}_{\theta}
and has an
\LL^{2}
-implementation
T_{i}\in\N
,

–

the net
(\Phi_{i})_{i\in I}
converges to the identity in the stable point-weak
∗
-topology.

Note that an
\LL^{2}
-implementation is unique. If it is clear from the context which weight on
\M
we choose, we will simply say that
\M
has W
∗
OAP relative to
\N
.

Theorem 6.16
.

Let
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
be a discrete quantum group. Consider the following conditions:

(1)

\mathbbold{\Gamma}
has AP.

(2)

\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold{\Gamma}})
has W
∗
OAP relative to
\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
.

(3)

\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold{\Gamma}})
has W
∗
OAP relative to
\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})^{\prime}
.

(4)

\mathbbold{\Gamma}
has central AP.

(5)

\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold{\Gamma}})
has W
∗
OAP relative to
\mathcal{Z}(\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
.

Then
(1)

\Leftrightarrow

(2)

\Leftrightarrow

(3)

\Leftarrow

(4)

\Leftrightarrow

(5)
.

Remark 6.17
.

This result is an analogue of Theorem 6.11 in
[
40
]
, for (co)amenability and relative W
∗
CPAP. Furthermore, it is similar in spirit to
[
63
, Theorem 3]
which is concerned with amenability and injectivity.

We start with an auxiliary result (compare
[
40
, Proposition 6.12]
and
[
62
, Proposition 2.12]
). Recall that for a normal CB map
\Phi
on a von Neumann algebra, we denote by
\Phi^{\dagger}
the normal CB map given by
x\mapsto\Phi(x^{*})^{*}
.

Proposition 6.18
.

Let
\mathbb{G}
be a locally compact quantum group with left Haar integral
\varphi
and let
\Phi\in\CB^{\sigma}(\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}))
be a normal CB map. Assume that
\Phi^{\dagger}
satisfies
\Phi^{\dagger}(\mathfrak{N}_{\widehat{\varphi}})\subseteq\mathfrak{N}_{\widehat{\varphi}}
and has an
\LL^{2}
-implementation
T\colon\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})
. We have:

(1)

T\in\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
if and only if
\Phi_{*}(\omega\star\nu)=\Phi_{*}(\omega)\star\nu
for all
\omega,\nu\in\operatorname{L}^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
,

(2)

T\in\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})^{\prime}
if and only if
\Phi_{*}(\omega\star\nu)=\omega\star\Phi_{*}(\nu)
for all
\omega,\nu\in\operatorname{L}^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
,

(3)

T\in\mathcal{Z}(\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}))
if and only if
\Phi_{*}(\omega\star\nu)=\Phi_{*}(\omega)\star\nu=\omega\star\Phi_{*}(\nu)
for all
\omega,\nu\in\operatorname{L}^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
.

Proof.

Using the biduality
\mathbb{G}=\widehat{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
and
[
70
, Definition 4.6]
(see also Section
2
), we deduce that the subspace

\mathcal{N}=\{\widehat{\lambda}(\omega)\,|\,\omega\in\operatorname{L}^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\colon\exists_{\xi\in\operatorname{L}^{2}(\mathbb{G})}\forall_{x\in\mathfrak{N}_{\widehat{\varphi}}}\langle\Lambda_{\widehat{\varphi}}(x)\,|\,\xi\rangle=\omega(x^{*})\}\subseteq\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})

is a core for
\Lambda_{\varphi}
, and that for
\widehat{\lambda}(\omega)\in\mathcal{N}
we have
\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\omega))=\xi
. Before we proceed with the main proof, let us establish some preliminary results:

•

For
\widehat{\lambda}(\omega)\in\mathcal{N}
we have
\widehat{\lambda}(\Phi_{*}(\omega))\in\mathcal{N}
and

(6.4)

T^{*}\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\omega))=\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\Phi_{*}(\omega))).

Indeed, for
x\in\mathfrak{N}_{\widehat{\varphi}}
,

\displaystyle\langle\Lambda_{\widehat{\varphi}}(x)\,|\,T^{*}\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\omega))\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle T\Lambda_{\widehat{\varphi}}(x)\,|\,\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\omega))\rangle=\langle\Lambda_{\widehat{\varphi}}(\Phi^{\dagger}(x))\,|\,\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\omega))\rangle

\displaystyle=\omega(\Phi^{\dagger}(x)^{*})=\omega(\Phi(x^{*}))=\Phi_{*}(\omega)(x^{*}),

which proves that
\widehat{\lambda}(\Phi_{*}(\omega))\in\mathcal{N}
and that equation (
6.4
) holds.

•

For
\omega\in\operatorname{L}^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}),\widehat{\lambda}(\nu)\in\mathcal{N}
we have
\widehat{\lambda}(\omega\star\nu)\in\mathcal{N}
and

(6.5)

\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\omega\star\nu))=\widehat{\lambda}(\omega)\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\nu)).

Indeed, take
x\in\mathfrak{N}_{\widehat{\varphi}}
. Using the definition of
\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
(equation (
2.1
)) we obtain

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\omega\star\nu)(x^{*})=\nu((\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta(x^{*}))=\nu((\overline{\omega}\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta(x)^{*})=\langle\Lambda_{\widehat{\varphi}}((\overline{\omega}\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta(x))\,|\,\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\nu))\rangle\\
&=\langle(\overline{\omega}\otimes\textnormal{id})({\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}*}})\Lambda_{\widehat{\varphi}}(x)\,|\,\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\nu))\rangle=\langle\Lambda_{\widehat{\varphi}}(x)\,|\,\widehat{\lambda}(\omega)\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\nu))\rangle,\end{split}

which proves the claim.

Let us now prove
(1)
. If
T\in\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
then (
6.4
) implies that
\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\Phi_{*}(\omega)))=T^{*}\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\omega))=\Lambda_{\varphi}(T^{*}\widehat{\lambda}(\omega))
and so
T^{*}\widehat{\lambda}(\omega)=\widehat{\lambda}(\Phi_{*}(\omega))
, for each
\omega\in\operatorname{L}^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
such that
\widehat{\lambda}(\omega)\in\mathcal{N}
, and by density of such
\omega
(see Lemma
2.1
) this equation holds for all
\omega\in\operatorname{L}^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
. Consequently

\widehat{\lambda}(\Phi_{*}(\omega\star\nu))=T^{*}\widehat{\lambda}(\omega\star\nu)=T^{*}\widehat{\lambda}(\omega)\widehat{\lambda}(\nu)=\widehat{\lambda}(\Phi_{*}(\omega))\widehat{\lambda}(\nu)=\widehat{\lambda}(\Phi_{*}(\omega)\star\nu),

and so
\Phi_{*}(\omega\star\nu)=\Phi_{*}(\omega)\star\nu
for all
\omega,\nu\in\operatorname{L}^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
.

For the converse, assume that
\Phi_{*}(\omega\star\nu)=\Phi_{*}(\omega)\star\nu
for all
\omega,\nu\in\operatorname{L}^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
. Let us take
\widehat{\lambda}(\omega),\widehat{\lambda}(\nu)\in\mathcal{N}
and assume that the map
\mathbb{R}\ni t\mapsto(\omega\hat{\delta}^{-it})\circ\hat{\tau}_{-t}\in\operatorname{L}^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
extends to an entire map, and denoting by
\rho
the value of this map at
t=-\tfrac{i}{2}
, we have furthermore
\widehat{\lambda}(\rho)\in\mathcal{N}
. We now use
{\mathrm{W}}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}=\chi(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}})^{*}
, together with
(\sigma^{\varphi}_{t}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}})=(\tau_{t}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}})(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\hat{\delta}^{it})
, see
[
70
, Proposition 5.15]
, and
(\tau_{t}\otimes\hat{\tau}_{t})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}})=\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}
, see
[
46
, Proposition 8.23]
. It follows that for each
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\begin{split}&\quad\;\sigma^{\varphi}_{t}(\widehat{\lambda}(\omega))=(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})((\textnormal{id}\otimes\sigma^{\varphi}_{t}){\mathrm{W}}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\overline{\omega})((\sigma^{\varphi}_{t}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}}))^{*}\\
&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\overline{\omega})((\tau_{t}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}})(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\hat{\delta}^{it}))^{*}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\overline{\omega})((\textnormal{id}\otimes\hat{\tau}_{-t})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}})(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\hat{\delta}^{it}))^{*}\\
&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega)((\mathbbm{1}\otimes\hat{\delta}^{-it})(\textnormal{id}\otimes\hat{\tau}_{-t})(\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}})^{*})=(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})((\hat{\delta}^{-it}\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\hat{\tau}_{-t}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}))\\
&=\widehat{\lambda}((\omega\hat{\delta}^{-it})\circ\hat{\tau}_{-t}).\end{split}

Consequently we obtain
\widehat{\lambda}(\omega)\in\operatorname{Dom}(\sigma^{\varphi}_{-i/2})
and
\sigma^{\varphi}_{-i/2}(\widehat{\lambda}(\omega))=\widehat{\lambda}(\rho)
. From (
6.5
), we know that
\widehat{\lambda}(\nu\star\rho),\widehat{\lambda}(\Phi_{*}(\nu)\star\rho)\in\mathcal{N}
. By assumption,
\Phi_{*}(\nu\star\rho)=\Phi_{*}(\nu)\star\rho
hence for
x\in\mathfrak{N}_{\widehat{\varphi}}
we have

\begin{split}&\quad\;\langle\Lambda_{\widehat{\varphi}}(x)\,|\,T^{*}J_{\varphi}\widehat{\lambda}(\omega)^{*}J_{\varphi}\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\nu))\rangle=\langle T\Lambda_{\widehat{\varphi}}(x)\,|\,\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\nu)\sigma^{\varphi}_{-i/2}(\widehat{\lambda}(\omega)))\rangle\\
&=\langle\Lambda_{\widehat{\varphi}}(\Phi^{\dagger}(x))\,|\,\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\nu\star\rho))\rangle=(\nu\star\rho)(\Phi^{\dagger}(x)^{*})=\Phi_{*}(\nu\star\rho)(x^{*})=(\Phi_{*}(\nu)\star\rho)(x^{*})\\
&=\langle\Lambda_{\widehat{\varphi}}(x)\,|\,\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\Phi_{*}(\nu)\star\rho))\rangle=\langle\Lambda_{\widehat{\varphi}}(x)\,|\,\Lambda_{\varphi}\bigl(\widehat{\lambda}(\Phi_{*}(\nu))\sigma^{\varphi}_{-i/2}(\widehat{\lambda}(\omega))\bigr)\rangle\\
&=\langle\Lambda_{\widehat{\varphi}}(x)\,|\,J_{\varphi}\widehat{\lambda}(\omega)^{*}J_{\varphi}T^{*}\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\nu))\rangle.\end{split}

By Lemma
2.1
we know that the collection of functionals
\omega
with
\widehat{\lambda}(\omega)\in\mathcal{N}
is dense in
\operatorname{L}^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
, hence the corresponding collection of operators
J_{\varphi}\widehat{\lambda}(\omega)^{*}J_{\varphi}
is weak
∗
-dense in
\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})^{\prime}
. Thus
T^{*}\in\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})^{\prime\prime}=\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})
, as required.

Next we consider
(2)
. Suppose that
T\in\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})^{\prime}
. By equations (
6.4
) and (
6.5
), given
\widehat{\lambda}(\omega),\widehat{\lambda}(\nu)\in\mathcal{N}
, we have

\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\Phi_{*}(\omega\star\nu)))=T^{*}\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\omega\star\nu))=T^{*}\widehat{\lambda}(\omega)\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\nu))=\widehat{\lambda}(\omega)T^{*}\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\nu))=\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\omega\star\Phi_{*}(\nu))),

hence
\Phi_{*}(\omega\star\nu)=\omega\star\Phi_{*}(\nu)
. As the set of functionals
\omega
with
\widehat{\lambda}(\omega)\in\mathcal{N}
is dense in
\operatorname{L}^{1}(\mathbb{G})
by Lemma
2.1
, the claim follows.

Conversely, suppose that
\Phi_{*}(\omega\star\nu)=\omega\star\Phi_{*}(\nu)
for all
\omega,\nu\in\operatorname{L}^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
. For
\widehat{\lambda}(\omega),\widehat{\lambda}(\nu)\in\mathcal{N}
we then have

\widehat{\lambda}(\omega)T^{*}\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\nu))=\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\omega\star\Phi_{*}(\nu)))=\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\Phi_{*}(\omega\star\nu)))=T^{*}\widehat{\lambda}(\omega)\Lambda_{\varphi}(\widehat{\lambda}(\nu)).

Again by density, it follows that
T^{*}\in\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{G})^{\prime}
, as required.

Finally,
(3)
follows by combining
(1)
and
(2)
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.16
.

If
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
has AP then by Proposition
6.5
we have a net
(a_{i})_{i\in I}
in
\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
which converges in
(\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma})),w^{*})
to
\mathbbm{1}
, and the associated maps
\Theta^{l}(a_{i})
satisfy
\Theta^{l}(a_{i})^{\dagger}=\Theta^{l}(a_{i})
. Proposition
4.12
shows that each
\Theta^{l}(a_{i})
has an
\LL^{2}
-implementation equal to
S^{-1}(a_{i})\in\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
. Proposition
4.7
, for
f=\varepsilon\in\ell^{1}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
being the counit of
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
, shows that
(\Theta^{l}(a_{i}))_{i\in I}
converges to the identity in the stable point-weak
∗
-topology. Indeed, for any Hilbert space
\mathsf{H}
and
x\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\bar{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H}),\omega\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\widehat{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H})_{*}
we have

\langle(\Theta^{l}(a_{i})\otimes\textnormal{id})x,\omega\rangle=\langle(\Theta^{l}(a_{i}\star\varepsilon)\otimes\textnormal{id})x,\omega\rangle=\langle a_{i},\Omega_{x,\omega,\varepsilon}\rangle\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\langle\mathbbm{1},\Omega_{x,\omega,\varepsilon}\rangle=\langle x,\omega\rangle.

We conclude that
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
has W
∗
OAP relative to
\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
. This shows
(1)

\Rightarrow

(2)
.

If
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
has central AP then additionally
S^{-1}(a_{i})\in\mathcal{Z}(\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
and so
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
has W
∗
OAP relative to
\mathcal{Z}(\ell^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}))
. Thus
(4)

\Rightarrow

(5)
.

Let us now show the equivalence of
(2)
and
(3)
. Assume
(2)
and let
(\Phi_{i})_{i\in I}
be a net giving W
∗
OAP of
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
relative to
\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
. Define a net
(\Psi_{i})_{i\in I}
by
\Psi_{i}=\widehat{R}\circ\Phi_{i}^{\dagger}\circ\widehat{R}
. Lemma
4.8
shows that each
\Psi_{i}
is a normal, finite rank CB map on
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
and
\Psi_{i}\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\textnormal{id}
in the stable point-weak
∗
-topology. Let
T_{i}\in\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
be the
\LL^{2}
-implementation of
\Phi_{i}
. By
[
47
, Proposition 2.11]
we know that
J_{\varphi}\Lambda_{h}(x)=\Lambda_{h}(\widehat{R}(x)^{*})
for each
x\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold{\Gamma}})
, and so

\begin{split}&\quad\;\Lambda_{h}(\Psi_{i}(x))=\Lambda_{h}\bigl(\widehat{R}\bigl(\Phi_{i}^{\dagger}(\widehat{R}(x))\bigr)\bigr)=J_{\varphi}\Lambda_{h}(\Phi_{i}^{\dagger}(\widehat{R}(x))^{*})\\
&=J_{\varphi}\Lambda_{h}(\Phi_{i}(\widehat{R}(x)^{*}))=J_{\varphi}T_{i}\Lambda_{h}(\widehat{R}(x)^{*})=J_{\varphi}T_{i}J_{\varphi}\Lambda_{h}(x).\end{split}

Hence
\Psi_{i}
has
\LL^{2}
-implementation
J_{\varphi}T_{i}J_{\varphi}\in\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})^{\prime}
, showing
(3)
. The converse is analogous.

Now assume
(2)
, i.e. that
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
has W
∗
OAP relative to
\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
. As before, let
(\Phi_{i})_{i\in I}
be the corresponding net of normal, finite rank CB maps with
\LL^{2}
-implementations
T_{i}\in\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
. For
i\in I
, let
\Psi=\Phi_{i}^{\dagger}
, so that
\Psi
is a normal CB map such that
\Psi^{\dagger}=\Phi_{i}
has an
\LL^{2}
-implementation
T_{i}\in\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
. By Proposition
6.18
(1)
,
\Psi_{*}
is a left centraliser. Hence also
\Phi_{i,*}
is a left centraliser, compare with the proof of Proposition
4.9
, and so there is
a_{i}\in\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
with
\Theta^{l}(a_{i})=\Phi_{i}
. By Lemma
6.1
, as
\Phi_{i}
is finite-rank, it must be that
a_{i}\in\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
. By definition,
(\Phi_{i})_{i\in I}=(\Theta^{l}(a_{i}))_{i\in I}
converges to the identity in the stable point-weak
∗
-topology, and so by Proposition
3.9
,
a_{i}\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\mathbbm{1}
in
(\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma})),w^{*})
and consequently
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
has AP.
Therefore
(1)
and
(2)
are equivalent.

Finally, suppose that
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
has W
∗
OAP relative to
\mathcal{Z}(\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
, and proceed as above. By definition, we have that
T_{i}\Lambda_{h}(x)=\Lambda_{h}(\Phi_{i}(x))=\Lambda_{h}(\Theta^{l}(a_{i})(x))
for each
x\in\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
. By Proposition
4.12
, it follows that
T_{i}=S(a_{i}^{*})^{*}
. As
T_{i}\in\mathcal{Z}(\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
, it follows that
a_{i}\in\mathcal{Z}(\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))\cap\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
, which shows that
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
has central AP. This establishes that
(4)
and
(5)
are equivalent. The implication
(4)

\Rightarrow

(1)
is trivial.
∎

7.
Permanence properties

7.1.
Quantum subgroups

For classical locally compact groups, AP passes to closed subgroups, see
[
32
, Proposition 1.14]
. We shall show that an analogous property holds also in the quantum case.

Let us start by recalling the notion of a closed quantum subgroup of a locally compact quantum group, see
[
20
]
. In what follows we will use the universal
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
\mathrm{C}^{u}_{0}(\mathbb{G})
and the reducing map
\Lambda_{\mathbb{G}}:\mathrm{C}^{u}_{0}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G})
, see
[
44
]
, along with the semi-universal and universal multiplicative unitaries, compare
[
20
, Section 1.2]
. We will also use the notion of a quantum homomorphism as explored in
[
50
]
, see also
[
19
, Section 2.1]
.

Let
\mathbb{H},\mathbb{G}
be locally compact quantum groups. Assume that there is a homomorphism
\mathbb{H}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}
exhibited by a
strong quantum homomorphism
in the sense
of
[
20
, Section 1.3]
, i.e. a non-degenerate
\star
-homomorphism
\pi:\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{G})\rightarrow\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\mathbb{H}))
such that
\Delta_{\mathbb{H}}^{u}\circ\pi=(\pi\otimes\pi)\circ\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}^{u}
.
Then there is a dual strong quantum homomorphism
\widehat{\pi}:\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\rightarrow\M(\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}))
which is related to
\pi
via
(\pi\otimes\textnormal{id}){\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{G}}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\widehat{\pi}){\mathds{V}\!\!\text{\reflectbox{$\mathds{V}$}}}^{\mathbb{H}}
, see
[
20
, Section 1.3]
. In this situation we say that
\mathbb{H}
is a
closed quantum subgroup
of
\mathbb{G}
in the sense of Vaes if there is a normal unital injective
\star
-homomorphism
\gamma\colon\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
such that
\gamma(\Lambda_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}(x))=\Lambda_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(\widehat{\pi}(x))
for all
x\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{u}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})
, see
[
67
, Definition 2.5]
,
[
20
, Definition 3.1]
. Notice that this condition implies that
\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\circ\gamma=(\gamma\otimes\gamma)\circ\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}
.

Theorem 7.1
.

Let
\mathbb{H},\mathbb{G}
be locally compact quantum groups and assume that
\mathbb{H}
is a closed quantum subgroup of
\mathbb{G}
in the sense of Vaes. If
\mathbb{G}
has AP then so does
\mathbb{H}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathbb{H}
is a closed quantum subgroup of
\mathbb{G}
we obtain maps
\pi,\widehat{\pi},\gamma
as discussed above. If
\mathbb{G}
has AP, then according to Theorem
4.4
we can choose a net
(a_{i})_{i\in I}
in
\A(\mathbb{G})\subseteq\mathrm{C}_{0}(\mathbb{G})
such that
(\Theta^{l}(a_{i}))_{i\in I}
converges to the identity in the stable point-weak
∗
-topology of
\CB^{\sigma}(\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}))
. For each
i\in I
let
\omega_{i}\in\operatorname{L}^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
be such that
a_{i}=\lambda_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(\omega_{i})
, and define
b_{i}=\lambda_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}(\gamma_{*}(\omega_{i}))
. As
\gamma_{*}(\omega_{i})\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})
, we see that
b_{i}\in\A(\mathbb{H})
.
Let
\mathsf{H}
be a separable Hilbert space and take
x\in\mathrm{C}_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\otimes\mathcal{K}(\mathsf{H}),\omega\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\widehat{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H})_{*}
. We have

\langle b_{i},\Omega_{x,\omega}\rangle=\langle(\Theta^{l}(b_{i})\otimes\textnormal{id})x,\omega\rangle=\langle\bigl((\gamma_{*}(\omega_{i})\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}\otimes\textnormal{id}\bigr)x,\omega\rangle.

Since
\gamma
is a complete isometry,
\gamma_{*}
is a complete quotient map (
[
23
, Corollary 4.1.9]
). By
[
23
, Proposition 7.1.7]
,
\gamma_{*}\otimes\textnormal{id}\colon\operatorname{L}^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\widehat{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H})_{*}\rightarrow\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\widehat{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H})_{*}
is also a complete quotient map, hence we can find
\omega^{\prime}\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\widehat{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H})_{*}
such that
\omega=(\gamma_{*}\otimes\textnormal{id})\omega^{\prime}
.

Since
\gamma
intertwines the coproducts,

\displaystyle\langle b_{i},\Omega_{x,\omega}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle(\omega_{i}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl((\gamma\otimes\gamma)\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}\otimes\textnormal{id}\bigr)x,\omega^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle((\omega_{i}\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\gamma\otimes\textnormal{id})x,\omega^{\prime}\rangle=\langle(\Theta^{l}(a_{i})\otimes\textnormal{id})(\gamma\otimes\textnormal{id})x,\omega^{\prime}\rangle.

Using stable point-weak
∗
-convergence we obtain

\langle b_{i},\Omega_{x,\omega}\rangle\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\langle(\gamma\otimes\textnormal{id})x,\omega^{\prime}\rangle=\langle x,\omega\rangle=\langle\mathbbm{1},\Omega_{x,\omega}\rangle.

Hence
b_{i}\xrightarrow[i\in I]{w^{*}}\mathbbm{1}
showing that
(b_{i})_{i\in I}
witnesses that
\mathbb{H}
has the AP.
∎

This argument also yields the analogous claim for weak amenability, which we record for completeness.

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
\mathbb{H},\mathbb{G}
be locally compact quantum groups and assume that
\mathbb{H}
is a closed quantum subgroup of
\mathbb{G}
in the sense of Vaes. If
\mathbb{G}
is weakly amenable, then so is
\mathbb{H}
, and
\Lambda_{cb}(\mathbb{H})\leq\Lambda_{cb}(\mathbb{G})
.

Proof.

We follow the same proof strategy, using also Proposition
5.7
. So, let
(a_{i})_{i\in I}
be a net in
\A(\mathbb{G})
with
\|a_{i}\|_{cb}\leq\Lambda_{cb}(\mathbb{G})
for each
i
, such that
(\Theta^{l}(a_{i}))_{i\in I}
converges to the identity in the stable point-weak
∗
-topology of
\CB^{\sigma}(\operatorname{L}^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}))
. Again let
a_{i}=\lambda_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}(\omega_{i})
, and define
b_{i}=\lambda_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}(\gamma_{*}(\omega_{i}))
. By the previous proof, and using Proposition
5.7
, it suffices to show that
\|b_{i}\|_{cb}\leq\Lambda_{cb}(\mathbb{G})
for each
i\in I
.

Let
\mathsf{H}
be a Hilbert space, and let
x\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\bar{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H})
and
\omega\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})\widehat{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H})_{*}
. Given
\varepsilon>0
, we can find
\omega^{\prime}\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})\widehat{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H})_{*}
with
\omega=(\gamma_{*}\otimes\textnormal{id})\omega^{\prime}
and with
\|\omega^{\prime}\|\leq\|\omega\|+\varepsilon
. Then, as before,

\displaystyle\langle(\Theta^{l}(b_{i})\otimes\textnormal{id})x,\omega\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle((\gamma_{*}(\omega_{i})\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{H}}}\otimes\textnormal{id})x,\omega\rangle=\langle((\omega_{i}\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\gamma\otimes\textnormal{id})x,\omega^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle(\Theta^{l}(a_{i})\otimes\textnormal{id})(\gamma\otimes\textnormal{id})x,\omega^{\prime}\rangle.

It follows that

\big|\langle(\Theta^{l}(b_{i})\otimes\textnormal{id})x,\omega\rangle\big|\leq\|a_{i}\|_{cb}\|x\|\|\omega^{\prime}\|.

As
\varepsilon>0
was arbitrary, and taking the supremum over functionals
\omega
with
\|\omega\|=1
, we obtain that
\|(\Theta^{l}(b_{i})\otimes\textnormal{id})x\|\leq\|a_{i}\|_{cb}\|x\|
. As
x,\mathsf{H}
were arbitrary, this shows that
\|b_{i}\|_{cb}\leq\|a_{i}\|_{cb}\leq\Lambda_{cb}(\mathbb{G})
, as required.
∎

7.2.
Direct limits

In this section we show that AP is preserved by taking direct limits of discrete quantum groups obtained from directed systems with injective connecting maps.
The corresponding fact for classical groups is certainly known, but we were not able to locate a reference.

Let us first recall some facts about quantum subgroups of discrete quantum groups, using the notation and terminology from Section
6
.
In the discrete setting there is no difference between closed quantum subgroups in the sense of Vaes, closed quantum subgroups in the sense of
Woronowicz
[
20
, Theorem 6.2]
, and open quantum subgroups in the sense of
[
36
]
.
We will therefore simply speak of quantum subgroups of discrete quantum groups in the sequel.

Let
\mathbbold{\Gamma},\mathbbold{\Lambda}
be discrete quantum groups and assume that
\mathbbold{\Lambda}
is a quantum subgroup of
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
.
Then one can identify
\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Lambda})
with a subset of
\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
, and one
obtains a corresponding identification of
\LL^{2}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Lambda})
with a subspace of
\LL^{2}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
.
Let
p\in\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})\subseteq\B(\LL^{2}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}))
be the projection onto
\LL^{2}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Lambda})
. Then
p
is a group-like
projection (i.e.
p
is a central projection in
\ell^{\infty}({\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\displaystyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\textstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{L}$}}}})
satisfying
\Delta_{\mathbbold{\Gamma}}(p)(\mathbbm{1}\otimes p)=p\otimes p
, see
[
36
, Definition 4.1]
) and the strong quantum homomorphism
\pi\colon\mathrm{c}_{0}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})\rightarrow\mathrm{c}_{0}(\mathbbold{\Lambda})
associated with the inclusion of
\mathbbold{\Lambda}
into
\mathbbold{\Gamma}

is given by
\pi(f)=fp
. Dually, we have an injective, normal,
unital
\star
-homomorphism
\iota\colon\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Lambda})\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
which respects the coproducts.
The map
\iota
restricts to injective
\star
-homomorphisms
\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Lambda})\rightarrow\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
and
\Pol(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Lambda})\rightarrow\Pol(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
.

The following fact is well-known, compare for instance
[
71
, Section 2]
. Using
\iota
we can view
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Lambda})
as a subalgebra of
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
, and so in the following, make sense of
\mathbb{E}
being a conditional expectation in the usual sense of a contractive projection onto a subalgebra.

Lemma 7.3
.

The formula
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\ni x\mapsto pxp\in\B(p\LL^{2}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}))=\B(\LL^{2}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Lambda}))
defines a normal conditional
expectation
\mathbb{E}\colon\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Lambda})

satisfying
\mathbb{E}(U^{\alpha}_{i,j})=0
for
\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\setminus\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Lambda}),\,1\leq i,j\leq\dim(\alpha)
.
Furthermore,
\mathbb{E}
restricts to a conditional expectation
\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\rightarrow\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Lambda})
.

We shall be interested in directed systems of discrete quantum groups in the following sense.

Definition 7.4
.

Let
I
be a directed set. A
directed system of discrete quantum groups with injective connecting maps
is a family of discrete quantum groups
(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i})_{i\in I}
together
with injective unital normal
\star
-homomorphisms

\iota_{j,i}\colon\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold{\Gamma}}_{i})\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold{\Gamma}}_{j})\qquad(i,j\in I\colon i\leq j),

compatible with coproducts, such that

•

\iota_{i,i}=\textnormal{id}
for
i\in I
,

•

\iota_{k,j}\iota_{j,i}=\iota_{k,i}
for all
i,j,k\in I
satisfying
i\leq j\leq k
.

If
(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i})_{i\in I}
is a directed system of discrete quantum groups with injective connecting maps then
\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}
is a quantum subgroup of
\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{j}
for
i\leq j
, and we have injective maps
\Pol(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i})\rightarrow\Pol(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{j})
.
The algebraic direct limit
\varinjlim_{i\in I}\Pol(\widehat{\mathbbold{\Gamma}}_{i})
becomes naturally a unital Hopf
*
-algebra, equipped with an invariant faithful state
induced by the Haar integrals of
\widehat{\mathbbold{\Gamma}}_{i}
.
We therefore have
\varinjlim_{i\in I}\Pol(\widehat{\mathbbold{\Gamma}}_{i})=\Pol(\widehat{\mathbbold{\Gamma}})
for a uniquely determined discrete quantum group
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
, see for example
[
38
, Chapter 11, Theorem 27]
. We denote
\mathbbold{\Gamma}=\varinjlim_{i\in I}\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}
and call this the
direct limit
of the directed system
(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i})_{i\in I}
.

Proposition 7.5
.

Let
(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i})_{i\in I}
be a directed system of discrete quantum groups with injective connecting maps and let
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
be its associated direct limit.
If
\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}
has (central) AP for all
i\in I
, then
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
has (central) AP.

Proof.

By construction each
\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}
is a quantum subgroup of
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
. Consequently we obtain injective normal
\star
-homomorphisms
\iota_{i}:\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold{\Gamma}}_{i})\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold{\Gamma}})
, and normal conditional expectations
\mathbb{E}_{i}\colon\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i})
for all
i\in I
.

Identifying
\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold{\Gamma}}_{i})
with a subset of
\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
gives us the extension by zero
*
-homomorphism map
\rho_{i}\colon\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i})\rightarrow\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
. For
\omega\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
we have
\omega\circ\iota_{i}\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i})
and so
\lambda_{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i}}(\omega\circ\iota_{i})\in\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i})
. We see that
\rho_{i}\lambda_{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i}}(\omega\circ\iota_{i})
agrees with
\lambda_{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}}(\omega)\in\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
restricted to
\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i})
and set to zero in the remaining matrix blocks. Similarly, for
\omega\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i})
, by normality,
\omega\circ\mathbb{E}_{i}\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
, and as
\mathbb{E}_{i}(U^{\alpha}_{i,j})=0
for
\alpha\not\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i})
, see Lemma
7.3
, it follows that
\lambda_{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}}(\omega\circ\mathbb{E}_{i})=\rho_{i}\lambda_{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i}}(\omega)
.

We claim that
\rho_{i}
restricts to a contraction
\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}))\rightarrow\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
. Indeed, take
a\in\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}))
and
\omega\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
. Using the observations from the previous paragraph,

\displaystyle\rho_{i}(a)\lambda_{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}}(\omega)

\displaystyle=\rho_{i}\bigl(a\lambda_{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i}}(\omega\circ\iota_{i})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\rho_{i}\bigl(\lambda_{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i}}(\Theta^{l}(a)_{*}(\omega\circ\iota_{i}))\bigr)=\rho_{i}\bigl(\lambda_{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i}}(\omega\circ\iota_{i}\circ\Theta^{l}(a))\bigr)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}}\bigl(\omega\circ\iota_{i}\circ\Theta^{l}(a)\circ\mathbb{E}_{i}\bigr).

It follows that
\rho_{i}(a)\in\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
and

\Theta^{l}(\rho_{i}(a))=\iota_{i}\circ\Theta^{l}(a)\circ\mathbb{E}_{i}\in\CB^{\sigma}(\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})),

which yields the claim.
By the definition of
\rho_{i}
it is clear that
\rho_{i}^{*}(\ell^{1}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))\subseteq\ell^{1}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i})\subseteq Q^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}))
, which shows that the induced map
\rho_{i}:\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}))\rightarrow\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
is weak
∗
-weak
∗
-continuous.

If
\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}
has AP then the identity element
\mathbbm{1}\in\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}))
is in the weak
∗
-closure of
\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i})
inside
\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}))
. As
\rho_{i}
is weak
∗
-weak
∗
-continuous, it follows that
\rho_{i}(\mathbbm{1})
is contained in the weak
∗
-closure of
\rho_{i}(\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}))\subseteq\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
. So
p_{i}=\rho_{i}(\mathbbm{1})
, the projection corresponding to
\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i})\subseteq\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
, is contained in the weak
∗
-closure of
\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
inside
\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
.
Clearly we have
\langle p_{i},\omega\rangle\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\langle\mathbbm{1},\omega\rangle
for all
\omega\in\ell^{1}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
.
Moreover we have
\|p_{i}\|_{cb}=1
since
p_{i}\in\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
with
\Theta^{l}(p_{i})=\iota_{i}\circ\mathbb{E}_{i}
.
Hence if all
\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}
have AP we see that
\mathbbm{1}\in\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
is contained in the weak
∗
-closure of
\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
. This means that
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
has AP.

If all
\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}
have central AP then we additionally know that
\mathbbm{1}\in\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}))
is in the weak
∗
-closure of
\mathcal{Z}(\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}))\cap\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i})
, hence each
p_{i}
is in the weak
∗
-closure of
\mathcal{Z}(\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))\cap\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
. It follows that
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
has central AP.
∎

7.3.
Free products

In this section we show that AP is preserved by the free product construction for discrete quantum groups. For classical groups this fact is probably known to experts, but
we could not find a proof in the literature. Our proof is based on results of Ricard and Xu from
[
58
]
which we recall first.

7.3.1.
Ricard-Xu results

Let
(A_{i},\phi_{i})_{i\in I}
be a family of unital
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebras with faithful states indexed by some set
I
. Denote by
\mathsf{H}_{i}
the GNS Hilbert space for
\phi_{i}
, and by
\mathsf{H}_{i}^{\operatorname{op}}
the Hilbert space obtained from
A_{i}
by completion with respect to the norm given by
a\mapsto\phi_{i}(aa^{*})^{1/2}
. Then
a\mapsto a^{*}
extends to an antilinear isometry
\mathsf{H}_{i}\rightarrow\mathsf{H}_{i}^{\operatorname{op}}
.

We write
\mathcal{A}=\star_{i\in I}(A_{i},\phi_{i})
for the
reduced unital free product
of the family
(A_{i},\phi_{i})_{i\in I}
, and
A\subseteq\mathcal{A}
for its canonical dense
unital
\star
-subalgebra (the algebraic unital free product), compare
[
5
]
. Next, for
d\geq 0
, denote by
\Sigma_{d}\subseteq A
the subspace of length-
d
elements.
That is, we have
\Sigma_{0}=\mathbb{C}\mathbbm{1}
, and if
\mathring{A}_{i}
denotes the set of all
a\in A_{i}
with
\phi_{i}(a)=0
,
then
\Sigma_{d}
for
d\geq 1
is the subspace of
A
spanned by all elements of the form
a_{1}\cdots a_{d}
where
a_{j}\in\mathring{A}_{i_{j}}
for each
j
, and with
i_{j}\neq i_{j+1}
for
1\leq j<d
.
Moreover we let
\mathcal{A}_{d}\subseteq\mathcal{A}
be the norm closure of
\Sigma_{d}
.

In the sequel we shall use two results from
[
58
]
, the first one being the following.

Lemma 7.6
.

[
58
, Corollary 3.3]

For
d\geq 0
, the natural projection
A\rightarrow\Sigma_{d}
onto length-
d
elements extends to a CB map
\mathcal{P}_{d}\colon\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{d}
with
\|\mathcal{P}_{d}\|_{cb}\leq\max(4d,1)
.

The second fact which we need is a minor extension of
[
58
, Lemma 4.10]
.

Lemma 7.7
.

Fix
d\geq 1
. For
i\in I
and
1\leq k\leq d
, let
T_{i,k}\in\CB(A_{i})
be linear maps which satisfy
\phi_{i}\circ T_{i,k}=\lambda_{i,k}\phi_{i}
for some
\lambda_{i,k}\in\mathbb{C}
, and which extend to bounded maps
\mathsf{H}_{i}\rightarrow\mathsf{H}_{i}
and
\mathsf{H}_{i}^{\operatorname{op}}\rightarrow\mathsf{H}_{i}^{\operatorname{op}}
. If

K=(2d+1)\prod_{k=1}^{d}\sup_{i}\max\bigl(\|T_{i,k}\|_{cb},\|T_{i,k}\|_{\B(\mathsf{H}_{i})},\|T_{i,k}\|_{\B(\mathsf{H}_{i}^{\operatorname{op}})}\bigr)<\infty

then the natural map
\Pi_{k}T_{i,k}\colon\Sigma_{d}\rightarrow\Sigma_{d}
given by

a_{1}\cdots a_{d}\mapsto T_{i_{1},1}(a_{1})\cdots T_{i_{d},d}(a_{d})\qquad(a_{j}\in\mathring{A}_{i_{j}},\;i_{j}\neq i_{j+1})

extends to a CB map
\mathcal{A}_{d}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{d}
with CB norm bounded above by
K
.

Proof.

The only difference between this claim and
[
58
, Lemma 4.10]
is that
[
58
, Lemma 4.10]
has the stronger hypothesis that
\phi_{i}\circ T_{i,k}=\phi_{i}
for each
i,k
. A close examination of the proof of
[
58
, Lemma 4.10]
shows that this hypothesis is only used to ensure that the map
\Pi_{k}T_{i,k}
is well-defined, because each
T_{i,k}
maps
\mathring{A}_{i}
to itself. This condition remains true under our weaker hypothesis, and the rest of the proof of
[
58
, Lemma 4.10]
carries over without change.
∎

7.3.2.
AP for free products

Let
\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{1},\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{2}
be discrete quantum groups and let
\mathbbold{\Gamma}=\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{1}\star\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{2}
be their
free product
. Recall from
[
73
]
that this means in particular
that
\mathcal{A}=\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
is the unital reduced free product
\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{1})\star\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{2})
with respect to Haar integrals,
and
h_{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}}
is the free product state
h_{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{1}}\star h_{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{2}}
. Moreover
\Pol(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
is the algebraic unital free product
of
\Pol(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{1})
and
\Pol(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{2})
. The irreducible representations of
\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}
are given as follows, see
[
73
, Theorem 3.10]
. Each
\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
has a well-defined length
\operatorname{len}(\alpha)\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. The trivial representation is the only representation of length
0
, and for
n\geq 1
we have

\{\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\,|\,\operatorname{len}(\alpha)=n\}=\{\alpha_{i_{1}}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\alpha_{i_{n}}\,|\,\forall_{1\leq j\leq n}\,\alpha_{i_{j}}\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i_{j}})\setminus\{e\},\;\forall_{1\leq j<n}\,i_{j}\neq i_{j+1}\}.

Again, here we denote by
e
the trivial representation of a compact quantum group.
More explicitly, given
\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{k})
associated to the representation matrix
U^{\alpha}=[U^{\alpha}_{i,j}]_{i,j=1}^{\dim(\alpha)}\in\M_{\dim(\alpha)}(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{k}))
, by regarding
\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{k})
as a subalgebra of
\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
, we may regard
U^{\alpha}
as a representation of
(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}),\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}})
. Then
\boxtimes
is just the usual tensor product of representations.

To ease notation, we will write
h=h_{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}}
in the sequel.

Theorem 7.8
.

Let
\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{1},\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{2}
be discrete quantum groups and let
\mathbbold{\Gamma}=\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{1}\star\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{2}
be their free product. If
\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{1},\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{2}
have (central) AP, then
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
has (central) AP.

Before we can prove Theorem
7.8
we need to establish some auxiliary results. For
d\in\mathbb{N}
let us define the (non-linear) map

(7.1)

\tilde{\Psi}_{d}\colon\bigoplus_{k=1}^{d}\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{1})\oplus_{\infty}\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{2})\rightarrow\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})

(here
\oplus
is the
\ell^{\infty}
-direct sum) via

\begin{split}\tilde{\Psi}_{d}((g_{1,k},g_{2,k})_{k=1}^{d})&=\bigl(\tilde{\Psi}_{d}((g_{1,k},g_{2,k})_{k=1}^{d})_{\alpha}\bigr)_{\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})},\qquad\text{where}\\
\tilde{\Psi}_{d}((g_{1,k},g_{2,k})_{k=1}^{d})_{\alpha}&=\begin{cases}0,&\textnormal{len}(\alpha)\neq d,\\
g_{i_{1},1,\alpha_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{i_{d},d,\alpha_{d}},&\alpha=\alpha_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\alpha_{d}\colon\alpha_{j}\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i_{j}}).\end{cases}\end{split}

We consider

\mathcal{V}=\bigoplus_{k=1}^{d}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{1}))\oplus_{\infty}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{2})),

and write
\Psi_{d}
for the restriction of
\tilde{\Psi}_{d}
to
\mathcal{V}
. Recall that
\mathcal{P}_{d}:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{d}
is induced by the projection onto elements of length
d
.

Lemma 7.9
.

The image of
\Psi_{d}
is a subset of
\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
, that is, we can regard
\Psi_{d}
as a map
\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
.
Furthermore,
\mathcal{P}_{d}
extends to a weak
∗
-weak
∗
-continuous CB map
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{A}_{d}}^{w^{*}}
.

Proof.

Fix
(g_{1,k},g_{2,k})_{k=1}^{d}\in\mathcal{V}
.
Proposition
4.12
shows that each
\Theta^{l}(g_{i,k})
extends to a bounded linear map on
\LL^{2}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i})
with norm
\|S_{\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}}^{-1}(g_{i,k})\|
.
Next, using Proposition
4.9
and Proposition
4.12
, for
x\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
, we have

\begin{split}&\quad\;\|\Theta^{l}(g_{i,k})(x)\|_{\LL^{2}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i})^{\operatorname{op}}}=\|\Theta^{l}(g_{i,k})(x)^{*}\|_{2}=\|\Theta^{l}(g_{i,k})^{\dagger}(x^{*})\|_{2}=\|\Theta^{l}(S_{\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}}(g_{i,k}^{*}))(x^{*})\|_{2}\\
&\leq\|S_{\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}}^{-1}(S_{\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}}(g_{i,k}^{*}))\|\|x^{*}\|_{2}=\|g_{i,k}^{*}\|\,\|x\|_{\LL^{2}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i})^{\operatorname{op}}}=\|g_{i,k}\|\,\|x\|_{\LL^{2}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i})^{\operatorname{op}}}.\end{split}

Hence
\Theta^{l}(g_{i,k})
extends to a bounded linear map on
\LL^{2}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i})^{\operatorname{op}}
with norm bounded by
\|g_{i,k}\|
.

Let us consider
T_{i,k}=\Theta^{l}(g_{i,k})\in\CB(\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i}))
for
1\leq k\leq d
. We have
h_{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i}}\circ T_{i,k}=g_{i,k,e}h_{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i}}
. Then, according to Lemma
7.6
and Lemma
7.7
, we obtain a
\CB
map
\Upsilon
on
\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold{\Gamma}})
acting by
0
on elements of length
d^{\prime}\neq d
, and on elements of length
d
by

a_{1}\cdots a_{d}\mapsto\Theta^{l}(g_{i_{1},1})(a_{1})\cdots\Theta^{l}(g_{i_{d},d})(a_{d}),

where
a_{j}\in\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i_{j}})
, and the
\CB
norm of
\Upsilon
is bounded above by

4d(2d+1)\prod_{k=1}^{d}\max_{i\in\{1,2\}}\max(\|g_{i,k}\|_{cb},\|S^{-1}_{\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}}(g_{i,k})\|,\|g_{i,k}\|).

Since
\|\cdot\|\leq\|\cdot\|_{cb}
on
\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}))
we have, using Lemma
4.8
and Proposition
4.9
,

\|S_{\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}}^{-1}(g_{i,k})\|=\|S_{\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}}^{-1}(g_{i,k})^{*}\|\leq\|S_{\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}}^{-1}(g_{i,k})^{*}\|_{cb}=\|g_{i,k}\|_{cb},

and hence we get in fact

(7.2)

\|\Upsilon\|_{cb}\leq 4d(2d+1)\prod_{k=1}^{d}\max_{i\in\{1,2\}}\|g_{i,k}\|_{cb}.

We claim that
\Upsilon
extends to a normal map on
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
. For this it suffices to show that
\Upsilon^{*}
preserves
\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\subseteq\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})^{*}
. Indeed, if this is the case, then the extension may be defined as
(\Upsilon^{*}|_{\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})})^{*}\in\CB^{\sigma}(\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}))
.

Thus take
\rho\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
. Since
\Upsilon
is bounded and
\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\subseteq\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})^{*}
is norm-closed, it is enough to consider
\rho=h(a\cdot)
for
a\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
. Take
b\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
and denote by
\Upsilon_{2}
the extension of
\Upsilon
to a bounded linear map on
\LL^{2}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
.
Note that this extension exists since the GNS Hilbert space for
h
is

\LL^{2}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})=\mathbb{C}\Omega\oplus\bigoplus_{d=1}^{\infty}\bigoplus_{i_{1}\neq\cdots\neq i_{d}}\LL^{2}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i_{1}})^{\circ}\otimes\cdots\otimes\LL^{2}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i_{d}})^{\circ},

where
\LL^{2}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i})^{\circ}
is the subspace of
\LL^{2}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i})
orthogonal to
\Lambda_{h_{i}}(\mathbbm{1})
(see
[
5
, Section 2]
),
and
\Theta^{l}(g_{i,k})
has bounded extension to
\LL^{2}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i})
by Proposition
4.12
. We have

\Upsilon^{*}(\rho)(b)=h(a\Upsilon(b))=\langle\Lambda_{h}(a^{*})\,|\,\Lambda_{h}(\Upsilon(b))\rangle=\langle\Lambda_{h}(a^{*})\,|\,\Upsilon_{2}\Lambda_{h}(b)\rangle=\langle\Upsilon_{2}^{*}\Lambda_{h}(a^{*})\,|\,\Lambda_{h}(b)\rangle.

Hence
\Upsilon^{*}(\rho)=\omega_{\Upsilon_{2}^{*}\Lambda_{h}(a^{*}),\Lambda_{h}(\mathbbm{1})}\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
.
Let us denote the resulting normal extension of
\Upsilon
to
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
with the same symbol.

In particular, taking
g_{i,k}=\mathbbm{1}
for all
1\leq k\leq d
in the above discussion shows that the projection
\mathcal{P}_{d}:\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{d}
extends to a normal CB map
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{A}_{d}}^{w^{*}}
.

We finally identify
\Upsilon
with the adjoint of a centraliser, namely we claim that
\Psi_{d}((g_{1,k},g_{2,k})_{k=1}^{d})\in\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
and
\Upsilon=\Theta^{l}(\Psi_{d}((g_{1,k},g_{2,k})_{k=1}^{d}))
. For
\alpha=\alpha_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\alpha_{d}\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
let us write
i_{j}\in\{1,2\}
for indices such that
\alpha_{j}\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i_{j}})\setminus\{e\},\,i_{j}\neq i_{j+1}
. Furthermore, write each matrix block as
g_{i,k,\alpha}=[g_{i,k,\alpha,m,n}]_{m,n=1}^{\dim(\alpha)}=\sum_{m,n=1}^{\dim(\alpha)}g_{i,k,\alpha,m,n}e^{\alpha}_{m,n}
, where
\{e^{\alpha}_{m,n}\}_{m,n=1}^{\dim(\alpha)}
are the matrix units in
\B(\mathsf{H}_{\alpha})
. Choose arbitrary
\omega\in h(\Pol(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\cdot)\subseteq\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
. We can calculate
\Psi_{d}((g_{1,k},g_{2,k})_{k=1}^{d})\lambda_{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}}(\omega)
as follows:

\begin{split}&\quad\;\Psi_{d}((g_{1,k},g_{2,k})_{k=1}^{d})\lambda_{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}}(\omega)\\
&=\sum_{d^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\alpha=\alpha_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\alpha_{d^{\prime}}}\sum_{m_{1},n_{1}=1}^{\dim(\alpha_{1})}\!\!\!\cdots\!\!\!\sum_{m_{d^{\prime}},n_{d^{\prime}}=1}^{\dim(\alpha_{d^{\prime}})}\!\!\langle U^{\alpha}_{(m_{1},\dotsc,m_{d^{\prime}}),(n_{1},\dotsc,n_{d^{\prime}})},\omega\rangle\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\Psi_{d}((g_{1,k},g_{2,k})_{k=1}^{d})(e^{\alpha_{1}}_{m_{1},n_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes e^{\alpha_{d^{\prime}}}_{m_{d^{\prime}},n_{d^{\prime}}})\\
&=\sum_{\alpha=\alpha_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\alpha_{d}}\sum_{m_{1},n_{1}=1}^{\dim(\alpha_{1})}\!\!\!\cdots\!\!\!\sum_{m_{d},n_{d}=1}^{\dim(\alpha_{d})}\!\!\langle U^{\alpha}_{(m_{1},\dotsc,m_{d}),(n_{1},\dotsc,n_{d})},\omega\rangle(g_{i_{1},1,\alpha_{1}}e^{\alpha_{1}}_{m_{1},n_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{i_{d},d,\alpha_{d}}e^{\alpha_{d}}_{m_{d},n_{d}})\\
&=\sum_{\alpha=\alpha_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\alpha_{d}}\sum_{m_{1},k_{1},n_{1}=1}^{\dim(\alpha_{1})}\!\!\!\cdots\!\!\!\sum_{m_{d},k_{d},n_{d}=1}^{\dim(\alpha_{d})}\!\!\langle U^{\alpha_{1}}_{m_{1},n_{1}}\cdots U^{\alpha_{d}}_{m_{d},n_{d}},\omega\rangle\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad(g_{i_{1},1,\alpha_{1},k_{1},m_{1}}e^{\alpha_{1}}_{k_{1},n_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{i_{d},d,\alpha_{d},k_{d},m_{d}}e^{\alpha_{d}}_{k_{d},n_{d}}),\end{split}

note that as
\omega\in h(\Pol(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\cdot)
, the sums above are finite. On the other hand, using Lemma
6.1
, we have

\begin{split}&\quad\;\lambda_{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}}(\Upsilon_{*}(\omega))\\
&=\sum_{d^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\alpha=\alpha_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\alpha_{d^{\prime}}}\sum_{m_{1},n_{1}=1}^{\dim(\alpha_{1})}\!\!\!\cdots\!\!\!\sum_{m_{d^{\prime}},n_{d^{\prime}}=1}^{\dim(\alpha_{d^{\prime}})}\!\!\langle U^{\alpha}_{(m_{1},\dotsc,m_{d^{\prime}}),(n_{1},\dotsc,n_{d^{\prime}})},\Upsilon_{*}(\omega)\rangle(e^{\alpha_{1}}_{m_{1},n_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes e^{\alpha_{d^{\prime}}}_{m_{d^{\prime}},n_{d^{\prime}}})\\
&=\sum_{\alpha=\alpha_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\alpha_{d}}\sum_{m_{1},n_{1}=1}^{\dim(\alpha_{1})}\!\!\!\cdots\!\!\!\sum_{m_{d},n_{d}=1}^{\dim(\alpha_{d})}\!\!\langle\Theta^{l}(g_{i_{1},1})(U^{\alpha_{1}}_{m_{1},n_{1}})\cdots\Theta^{l}(g_{i_{d},d})(U^{\alpha_{d}}_{m_{d},n_{d}}),\omega\rangle(e^{\alpha_{1}}_{m_{1},n_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes e^{\alpha_{d}}_{m_{d},n_{d}})\\
&=\!\!\sum_{\alpha=\alpha_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\alpha_{d}}\sum_{m_{1},k_{1},n_{1}=1}^{\dim(\alpha_{1})}\!\!\!\cdots\!\!\!\sum_{m_{d},k_{d},n_{d}=1}^{\dim(\alpha_{d})}\!\!\langle(g_{i_{1},1,m_{1},k_{1}}U^{\alpha_{1}}_{k_{1},n_{1}})\!\cdots\!(g_{i_{d},d,m_{d},k_{d}}U^{\alpha_{d}}_{k_{d},n_{d}}),\omega\rangle(e^{\alpha_{1}}_{m_{1},n_{1}}\otimes\!\cdots\!\otimes e^{\alpha_{d}}_{m_{d},n_{d}})\end{split}

These two computations show that
\Psi_{d}((g_{1,k},g_{2,k})_{k=1}^{d})\lambda_{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}}(\omega)=\lambda_{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}}(\Upsilon_{*}(\omega))
. As the space of functionals
h(\Pol(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\cdot)
is dense in
\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
, this proves the claim.
∎

Define

\mathcal{V}_{0}=\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{1}))\oplus_{\infty}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{2}))

so that
\mathcal{V}=\bigoplus_{k=1}^{d}\mathcal{V}_{0}
.

Note that
\mathcal{V}=\bigoplus_{k=1}^{d}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{1}))\oplus_{\infty}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{2}))
is a dual Banach space with predual given by
the
\ell^{1}
-direct sum
\bigoplus_{k=1}^{d}Q^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{1}))\oplus_{1}Q^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{2}))
. Similarly,
\mathcal{V}_{0}
is a dual Banach space with predual
Q^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{1}))\oplus_{1}Q^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{2}))
.

Lemma 7.10
.

Map
\Psi_{d}
is separately weak
∗
-weak
∗
-continuous, i.e. for any
1\leq k\leq d
and fixed elements
(g_{1,k^{\prime}},g_{2,k^{\prime}})\in\mathcal{V}_{0}
for
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq d
and
k^{\prime}\neq k
, the map

\mathcal{V}_{0}\ni(g_{1,k},g_{2,k})\mapsto\Psi_{d}((g_{1,k^{\prime}},g_{2,k^{\prime}})_{k^{\prime}=1}^{d})\in\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))

is weak
∗
-weak
∗
-continuous.

Proof.

Fix
1\leq k\leq d
and
(g_{1,k^{\prime}},g_{2,k^{\prime}})\in\mathcal{V}_{0}
for
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq d
and
k^{\prime}\neq k
. Assume that

(g_{1,k}^{\lambda},g_{2,k}^{\lambda})\xrightarrow[\lambda\in\Lambda]{w^{*}}(g_{1,k},g_{2,k})\quad\textnormal{ in }\quad\mathcal{V}_{0}=\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{1}))\oplus_{\infty}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{2})),

in particular

(g_{1,k}^{\lambda},g_{2,k}^{\lambda})\xrightarrow[\lambda\in\Lambda]{w^{*}}(g_{1,k},g_{2,k})\quad\textnormal{ in }\quad\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{1})\oplus_{\infty}\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{2}).

Take
\Omega\in Q^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
.
Since
Q^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
is the norm closure of
\ell^{1}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
in
\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))^{*}
, we can find a sequence
(\Omega_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
in
\ell^{1}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})\subseteq Q^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
which converges in norm to
\Omega
. By the description (
7.1
) of
\tilde{\Psi}_{d}
, we see that the map

\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{1})\oplus_{\infty}\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{2})\ni(g_{1,k}^{\prime},g_{2,k}^{\prime})\mapsto\tilde{\Psi}_{d}\bigl((g_{1,k^{\prime}},g_{2,k^{\prime}})_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1},(g^{\prime}_{1,k},g_{2,k}^{\prime}),(g_{1,k^{\prime}},g_{2,k^{\prime}})_{k^{\prime}=k+1}^{d}\bigr)\in\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})

is weak
∗
-weak
∗
-continuous, hence the linear functional

\Omega_{n}\circ\tilde{\Psi}_{d}\bigl((g_{1,k^{\prime}},g_{2,k^{\prime}})_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1},\;\cdot\;,(g_{1,k^{\prime}},g_{2,k^{\prime}})_{k^{\prime}=k+1}^{d}\bigr)

is contained in
\ell^{1}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{1})\oplus_{1}\ell^{1}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{2})
for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Consider the difference

\begin{split}\Omega_{n}&\circ\Psi_{d}\bigl((g_{1,k^{\prime}},g_{2,k^{\prime}})_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1},\;\cdot\;,(g_{1,k^{\prime}},g_{2,k^{\prime}})_{k^{\prime}=k+1}^{d}\bigr)\\
-\Omega&\circ\Psi_{d}\bigl((g_{1,k^{\prime}},g_{2,k^{\prime}})_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1},\;\cdot\;,(g_{1,k^{\prime}},g_{2,k^{\prime}})_{k^{\prime}=k+1}^{d}\bigr),\end{split}

living in
\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{1}))^{*}\oplus_{1}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{2}))^{*}
. Because of the bound (
7.2
) we can estimate

\begin{split}&\quad\;\bigl\|(\Omega-\Omega_{n})\circ\Psi_{d}\bigl((g_{1,k^{\prime}},g_{2,k^{\prime}})_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1},\;\cdot\;,(g_{1,k^{\prime}},g_{2,k^{\prime}})_{k^{\prime}=k+1}^{d}\bigr)\bigr\|\\
&=\sup_{(g^{\prime}_{1,k},g^{\prime}_{2,k})\in(\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{1}))\oplus_{\infty}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{2})))_{1}}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\bigl|(\Omega-\Omega_{n})\circ\Psi_{d}\bigl((g_{1,k^{\prime}},g_{2,k^{\prime}})_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1},(g^{\prime}_{1,k},g^{\prime}_{2,k}),(g_{1,k^{\prime}},g_{2,k^{\prime}})_{k^{\prime}=k+1}^{d}\bigr)\bigr|\\
&\leq\|\Omega-\Omega_{n}\|\;4d(2d+1)\prod_{k^{\prime}=1,k^{\prime}\neq k}^{d}\max_{i\in\{1,2\}}\|g_{i,k^{\prime}}\|_{cb}\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}0.\end{split}

This shows

\Omega\circ\Psi_{d}\bigl((g_{1,k^{\prime}},g_{2,k^{\prime}})_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1},\;\cdot\;,(g_{1,k^{\prime}},g_{2,k^{\prime}})_{k^{\prime}=k+1}^{d}\bigr)\in Q^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{1}))\oplus_{1}Q^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{2})),

and hence

\Omega\circ\Psi_{d}\bigl((g_{1,k^{\prime}},g_{2,k^{\prime}})_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1},(g_{1,k}^{\lambda},g_{2,k}^{\lambda}),(g_{1,k^{\prime}},g_{2,k^{\prime}})_{k^{\prime}=k+1}^{d}\bigr)\xrightarrow[\lambda\in\Lambda]{}\Omega\circ\Psi_{d}\bigl((g_{1,k^{\prime}},g_{2,k^{\prime}})_{k^{\prime}=1}^{d}\bigr)

as desired. This proves that
\Psi_{d}
is separately weak
∗
-weak
∗
-continuous.
∎

For
d\geq 1
consider
p_{d}\in\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
defined via
p_{d}=(p_{d,\alpha})_{\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})}
where

p_{d,\alpha}=\begin{cases}0,&\textnormal{length of }\alpha\neq d,\\
\mathbbm{1},&\textnormal{length of }\alpha=d.\end{cases}

Lemma 7.11
.

Projection
p_{d}
belongs to
\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
and
\Theta^{l}(p_{d})=\mathcal{P}_{d}
.

Proof.

We already know that
\mathcal{P}_{d}
is a weak
∗
-continuous map on
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
. Take a linear functional
\omega\in h(\Pol(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\,\cdot)\subseteq\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
. Then
we get

\begin{split}&\quad\;(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl((\mathbbm{1}\otimes p_{d})\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}}\bigr)=\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}):\;\operatorname{len}(\alpha)=d^{\prime}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{\dim(\alpha)}(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl(U^{\alpha}_{i,j}\otimes p_{d}\,e^{\alpha}_{i,j}\bigr)\\
&=\sum_{\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}):\;\operatorname{len}(\alpha)=d}\sum_{i,j=1}^{\dim(\alpha)}(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl(U^{\alpha}_{i,j}\otimes e^{\alpha}_{i,j}\bigr)\\
&=\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}):\;\operatorname{len}(\alpha)=d^{\prime}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{\dim(\alpha)}(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl(\mathcal{P}_{d}(U^{\alpha}_{i,j})\otimes e^{\alpha}_{i,j}\bigr)=(\omega\circ\mathcal{P}_{d}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}},\end{split}

noting that all sums in this calculation are finite, because of the form of
\omega
. As such
\omega
are dense, this yields
(\mathbbm{1}\otimes p_{d})\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}}=(\mathcal{P}_{d}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}}
as required.
∎

Finally, we are ready to prove that AP is preserved by taking free products of discrete quantum groups.

Proof of Theorem
7.8
.

Assume that
\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{1},\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{2}
have AP and for
i\in\{1,2\}
choose families
(f_{i,\lambda})_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{i}}
in
\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i})
converging to
\mathbbm{1}
in
(\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i})),w^{*})
.
Due to Proposition
6.5
we may assume without loss of generality that each
\Theta^{l}(f_{i,\lambda})
is unit preserving. As in Remark
6.2
, it then follows that
\Theta^{l}(f_{i,\lambda})
preserves the Haar integral on
\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}_{i}
, and that
f_{i,\lambda,e}=1
for all
i\in\{1,2\},\lambda\in\Lambda_{i}
.

Fix
d\in\mathbb{N}
. We shall first show that
p_{d}\in\overline{\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})}^{w^{*}}\subseteq\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
. To do this, we will consider a net of the form

(f_{1,\lambda_{1,k}},f_{2,\lambda_{2,k}})_{k=1}^{d}\in\mathcal{V}.

where each
\lambda_{i,k}\in\Lambda_{i}
for
i\in\{1,2\}
and
1\leq k\leq d
.
Lemma
7.10
gives us

\Psi_{d}\bigl((f_{1,\lambda_{1,k}},f_{2,\lambda_{2,k}})_{k=1}^{d}\bigr)\in\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma})).

In fact, using the definition of
\tilde{\Psi}_{d}
, we see that these multipliers are in
\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
since all of the
f_{i,\lambda}
are finitely supported.

We first consider the case when we keep
\lambda_{i,k}
fixed, for
k\geq 2
. Since
\Psi_{d}
is separately weak
∗
-weak
∗
-continuous, by Lemma
7.10
, we have

\Psi_{d}\bigl((f_{1,\lambda_{1,1}},f_{2,\lambda_{2,1}}),(f_{1,\lambda_{1,k}},f_{2,\lambda_{2,k}})_{k=2}^{d}\bigr)\xrightarrow[(\lambda_{1,1},\lambda_{2,1})\in\Lambda_{1}\times\Lambda_{2}]{w^{*}}\Psi_{d}\bigl((\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1}),(f_{1,\lambda_{1,k}},f_{2,\lambda_{2,k}})_{k=2}^{d}\bigr).

We now repeat this argument in the second variable, and so forth, and using that
(\overline{\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})}^{w^{*}})^{-w^{*}}=\overline{\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})}^{w^{*}}
, we obtain

p_{d}=\Psi_{d}((\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1})_{k=1}^{d})\in\overline{\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})}^{w^{*}}\subseteq\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma})).

Clearly we also have
p_{0}=(\delta_{\alpha,e}\mathbbm{1})_{\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})}\in\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})\subseteq\overline{\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})}^{w^{*}}
.

We now show that
\mathbbm{1}\in\overline{\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})}^{w^{*}}
. Consider

T_{n}=\sum_{d=0}^{n}(1-\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{n}})^{d}p_{d}\in\overline{\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})}^{w^{*}}\subseteq\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))\qquad(n\in\mathbb{N}).

According to
[
58
, Proposition 3.5]
,
\lim_{n\to\infty}\|T_{n}\|_{cb}=1
and
(\Theta^{l}(T_{n}))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges pointwise to the identity on
\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
. Take
x\in\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\odot\mathcal{K}(\mathsf{H}),\omega\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\odot\B(\mathsf{H})_{*}
for a separable Hilbert space
\mathsf{H}
. Then we obtain

\langle T_{n}-\mathbbm{1},\Omega_{x,\omega}\rangle=\langle\bigl((\Theta^{l}(T_{n})-\textnormal{id})\otimes\textnormal{id}\bigr)x,\omega\rangle\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}0,

and since
(T_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is uniformly bounded in CB norm, the same holds for general
x\in\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\otimes\mathcal{K}(\mathsf{H}),\omega\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\widehat{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H})_{*}
. By Proposition
3.9
, this shows that
T_{n}\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}\mathbbm{1}
weak
∗
in
\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
. As each
p_{d}
is in the weak
∗
-closure of
\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
, the same is true of each
T_{n}
, and hence we conclude that
\mathbbm{1}
is in the weak
∗
-closure of
\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
, showing that
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
has AP.

If
f_{i,\lambda}\in\mathcal{Z}(\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}))\cap\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i})
for each
i,\lambda
, then
\Psi_{d}((f_{1,\lambda_{1,k}},f_{2,\lambda_{2,k}})_{k=1}^{d})
is also central. Consequently, if
\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{1},\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{2}
have central AP then so does
\mathbbold{\Gamma}=\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{1}\star\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{2}
.
∎

Corollary 7.12
.

Let
(\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i})_{i\in I}
be a family of discrete quantum groups with (central) AP. Then the free product
\mathbbold{\Gamma}=\star_{i\in I}\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}
has (central) AP.

Proof.

If
I
is finite the claim follows from Theorem
7.8
by induction. In the general case, for any finite (nonempty) set
F\subseteq I
, the free product
\star_{i\in F}\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}
is a quantum subgroup of
\star_{i\in I}\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}
in a natural way. Moreover
(\star_{i\in F}\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i})_{F\subseteq I}
forms a directed system of discrete quantum groups with injective connecting maps over the directed set of finite subsets of
I
, compare Definition
7.4
, and
\star_{i\in I}\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}=\varinjlim_{F\subseteq I}\star_{i\in F}\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}
. Since
\star_{i\in F}\mathbbold{\Gamma}_{i}
has (central) AP, the claim follows from Proposition
7.5
.
∎

7.4.
Double crossed products

In this section we study how the approximation property behaves with respect to the double crossed product construction. This contains the Drinfeld double of
a locally compact quantum group as a special case.

7.4.1.
Preliminaries

We start by recalling some definitions, following the conventions in
[
6
]
.

A
matching
between two locally compact quantum groups
\mathbb{G}_{1},\mathbb{G}_{2}
is a faithful normal
\star
-homomorphism
\operatorname{m}\colon\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2})
satisfying
