(\Delta_{1}\otimes\textnormal{id})\operatorname{m}=\operatorname{m}_{23}\operatorname{m}_{13}(\Delta_{1}\otimes\textnormal{id})\quad\textnormal{ and }\quad(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Delta_{2})\operatorname{m}=\operatorname{m}_{13}\operatorname{m}_{12}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Delta_{2}).

Given this data one defines the
double crossed product

\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}
of
\mathbb{G}_{1},\mathbb{G}_{2}
as follows. The Hilbert space of square integrable functions on
\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}
, the von Neumann algebra of functions on
\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}
and its comultiplication are given by

\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}})=\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{2}),\;\,\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}})=\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2}),\;\,\Delta_{\operatorname{m}}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\chi\operatorname{m}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\Delta_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}\otimes\Delta_{2}).

To ease notation, we will decorate objects related to
\mathbb{G}_{1}
(resp.
\mathbb{G}_{2},\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}})
with
1
(resp.
2,\operatorname{m}
) in the sequel, e.g.
\mathrm{W}_{1}=\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{G}_{1}}
. We will also denote the unit in
\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}))
by
\mathbbm{1}_{\operatorname{m}}
.

Let
J
(resp.
\hat{J}
) be the modular conjugation of the left Haar integral on the bicrossed product of
\mathbb{G}_{1},\mathbb{G}_{2}
(resp. its dual), see
[
6
, Section 2.4]
.
Define a unitary

Z=J\hat{J}(\hat{J}_{1}J_{1}\otimes\hat{J}_{2}J_{2}).

It implements
\operatorname{m}
in the sense that
\operatorname{m}(z)=ZzZ^{*}
for all
z\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2})
. The Kac-Takesaki operator of
\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}
is given by

\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}=(\Sigma\mathrm{V}_{1}^{*}\Sigma)_{13}Z_{34}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{2,24}Z_{34}.

One can describe structure of
\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}
and its dual, see in particular
[
6
, Theorem 5.3]
. For example, we have

\begin{split}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}})&=\bigl((\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}})^{\prime}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\cup Z^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{2}}))Z\bigr)^{\prime\prime},\\
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}})^{\prime}&=\bigl(Z^{*}(\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}})\otimes\mathbbm{1})Z\cup(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{2}})^{\prime})\bigr)^{\prime\prime}.\end{split}

A special case of this construction is the (generalised) Drinfeld double. Let
\mathbb{G}_{1},\mathbb{G}_{2}
be locally compact quantum groups
and assume that
\mathcal{Z}\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2})
is a bicharacter. That is,
\mathcal{Z}
is a unitary satisfying

(\Delta_{1}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{Z}_{23}\mathcal{Z}_{13}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Delta_{2})\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{Z}_{13}\mathcal{Z}_{12}.

Then one obtains an inner
\star
-automorphism

\operatorname{m}\colon\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2})\ni x\mapsto\mathcal{Z}x\mathcal{Z}^{*}\in\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2}),

and it is easy to check that this defines a matching between
\mathbb{G}_{1}
and
\mathbb{G}_{2}
.
Consequently, one can form the double crossed product
\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}
, and this is called the
generalised Drinfeld double
of
\mathbb{G}_{1},\mathbb{G}_{2}
with respect to
\mathcal{Z}
.

In particular, if
\mathbb{H}
is a locally compact quantum group then we can consider
\mathbb{G}_{1}=\mathbb{H}^{\operatorname{op}},\mathbb{G}_{2}=\widehat{\mathbb{H}}
together with the bicharacter
\mathcal{Z}=\mathrm{W}^{\mathbb{H}}
.
The corresponding double crossed product
\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}
is called the
Drinfeld double
of
\mathbb{H}
.

7.4.2.
\mathbb{G}_{1}^{\operatorname{op}},\mathbb{G}_{2}
are quantum subgroups of
\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}

Let us return to the general situation of locally compact quantum groups
\mathbb{G}_{1},\mathbb{G}_{2}
with a matching
\operatorname{m}
. It is stated in
[
6
, Theorem 5.3]
, see also the introduction to
[
6
, Section 6]
, that
\mathbb{G}_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}
and
\mathbb{G}_{2}
are closed quantum subgroups of
\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}
. We give a quick argument for the convenience of the reader.

Lemma 7.13
.

\mathbb{G}_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}
and
\mathbb{G}_{2}
are closed quantum subgroups of
\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}
in the sense of Vaes.

Proof.

Note first that
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}}=\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}}^{\prime}
, compare
[
47
, Proposition 5.4]
. We have natural normal, injective
\star
-homomorphisms

\begin{split}\gamma_{1}&\colon\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}})^{\prime}\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}})\colon\widehat{x}^{\prime}\mapsto\widehat{x}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbbm{1},\\
\gamma_{2}&\colon\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{2}})\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}})\colon\widehat{x}\mapsto Z^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\widehat{x})Z,\end{split}

hence by
[
20
, Theorem 3.3]
it is enough to show that both maps respect coproducts.

First, take
\widehat{x}^{\prime}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}})^{\prime}
. Then
\widehat{x}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}})
and

\widehat{\Delta}_{\operatorname{m}}(\widehat{x}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbbm{1})=\Sigma\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}((\widehat{x}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\operatorname{m}})\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}^{*}\Sigma=\Sigma(\Sigma\mathrm{V}_{1}^{*}\Sigma)_{13}(\widehat{x}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\Sigma\mathrm{V}_{1}\Sigma)_{13}\Sigma,

noting that all the other parts of
\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}
cancel out. By slight abuse of notation, we write here
\Sigma
both for the swap map on
\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}})\otimes\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}})
,
which is identified with
\Sigma_{13}\Sigma_{24}
, and for the swap map on
\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\otimes\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1})
.
From the proof of
[
47
, Proposition 4.2]
we find that the coproduct on
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}})^{\prime}
is given by
\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}}^{\prime}}(\widehat{x}^{\prime})=\mathrm{V}_{1}^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\widehat{x}^{\prime})\mathrm{V}_{1}
, and so

\widehat{\Delta}_{\operatorname{m}}(\widehat{x}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbbm{1})=\Sigma(\Sigma\mathrm{V}_{1}^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\widehat{x}^{\prime})\mathrm{V}_{1}\Sigma)_{13}\Sigma=\Sigma_{24}\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}}^{\prime}}(\widehat{x}^{\prime})_{13}\Sigma_{24}=\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}}^{\prime}}(\widehat{x}^{\prime})_{13}.

Since the inclusion
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}})^{\prime}\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}})^{\prime}\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}})
is given by
a\mapsto a_{13}
, this concludes the proof that
\mathbb{G}_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}
is a
closed quantum subgroup of
\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}
.

Take now
\widehat{x}\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{2}})
so that
Z^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\widehat{x})Z\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}})
. Then, following exactly the proof of
[
6
, Proposition 3.5]
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\Delta}_{\operatorname{m}}(Z^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\widehat{x})Z)

\displaystyle=\Sigma\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}(Z^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\widehat{x})Z\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\operatorname{m}})\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}^{*}\Sigma=(Z^{*}\otimes Z^{*})\widehat{\Delta}_{2}(\widehat{x})_{24}(Z\otimes Z),

which is exactly the embedding of
\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{2}}}(\widehat{x})\in\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{2}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{2}})
into
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}})
.
∎

As a consequence of Theorem
7.1
and Proposition
4.3
we therefore obtain the following fact.

Corollary 7.14
.

Suppose that
\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}
has AP. Then both
\mathbb{G}_{1}
and
\mathbb{G}_{2}
have AP.

Remark 7.15
.

In view of the close analogy between Drinfeld doubles of
q
-deformations of compact semisimple Lie groups and the corresponding complex Lie groups
[
1
]
,
[
72
]
,
it is natural to speculate that the converse to Corollary
7.14
does not hold, see also Remark 7.4 in
[
2
]
.
Specifically, the Drinfeld double of
\SU_{q}(3)
might be an example of a locally compact quantum group which does not have AP.

7.4.3.
AP for
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}}

We now aim to prove the following result.

Theorem 7.16
.

Let
\mathbb{G}_{1},\mathbb{G}_{2}
be locally compact quantum groups with a matching
\operatorname{m}
.

•

If
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}}
and
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{2}}
have AP then so does
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}}
.

•

If
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}}
and
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{2}}
are weakly amenable with Cowling–Haagerup constants
\Lambda_{cb}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}}),\Lambda_{cb}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{2}})
then
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}}
is weakly amenable with
\Lambda_{cb}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}})\leq\Lambda_{cb}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}})\,\Lambda_{cb}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{2}})
.

•

If
\mathbb{G}_{1},\mathbb{G}_{2}
are coamenable then so is
\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}
.

An analogous result for the Haagerup property was obtained in
[
59
]
, but we note that the terminology used in
[
59
]
is different.
We also note that for generalised Drinfeld doubles the statement on coamenability in Theorem
7.16
can be shown quite easily using standard properties of bicharacters,
as discussed in a preprint version of
[
59
]
.

Before we prove Theorem
7.16
we need to establish a number of auxiliary results. Recall from Section
3.3
the
construction of a normal CB map
\Theta^{l}(a)
on
\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{H}})
and its extension
\Phi(a)\in\CB^{\sigma}(\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{H})))
for any locally compact
quantum group
\mathbb{H}
and
a\in\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbb{H}))
.
Moreover, in the proof of Lemma
7.13
we introduced injective, normal
\star
-homomorphisms
\gamma_{1}\colon\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}})^{\prime}\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}})

and
\gamma_{2}\colon\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{2}})\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}})
. We will now use these maps to transport elements of
the Fourier algebras
\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}}),\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{2}})
to left CB multipliers of
\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}})
.

Lemma 7.17
.

For
\omega\in\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G}_{1})
we have
\gamma_{1}(\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega))\in\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}}))
. The maps associated with
a=\gamma_{1}(\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega))
are

\Theta^{l}(a)=\Theta^{l}(\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega))\otimes\textnormal{id}\in\CB^{\sigma}(\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}))=\CB^{\sigma}(\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{1})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))

and

\Phi(a)=\Phi(\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega))\otimes\textnormal{id}\in\CB^{\sigma}(\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}})))=\CB^{\sigma}(\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1}))\bar{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))).

Proof.

Take
\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2}\in\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G}_{1}^{\operatorname{op}})\widehat{\otimes}\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G}_{2})=\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}})
. Using
\mathrm{W}_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}=\Sigma\mathrm{V}_{1}^{*}\Sigma
, see
[
47
, Section 4]
, we get

(7.3)

\begin{split}\lambda_{\operatorname{m}}(\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2})&=(\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})\bigl((\Sigma\mathrm{V}_{1}^{*}\Sigma)_{13}Z_{34}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{2,24}Z_{34}\bigr)\\
&=\bigl((\omega_{1}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\Sigma\mathrm{V}_{1}^{*}\Sigma)\otimes\mathbbm{1}\bigr)Z^{*}\bigl(\mathbbm{1}\otimes(\omega_{2}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}_{2})\bigr)Z\\
&=\gamma_{1}(\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega_{1}))\gamma_{2}(\lambda_{2}(\omega_{2})),\end{split}

and consequently, writing
\star
for the product on
\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G}_{1}^{\operatorname{op}})
,

\displaystyle a\lambda_{\operatorname{m}}(\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2})

\displaystyle=\gamma_{1}(\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega))\lambda_{\operatorname{m}}(\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2})=\gamma_{1}\big(\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega)\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega_{1})\big)\gamma_{2}(\lambda_{2}(\omega_{2}))

\displaystyle=\gamma_{1}\big(\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega\star\omega_{1})\big)\gamma_{2}(\lambda_{2}(\omega_{2}))=\lambda_{\operatorname{m}}\bigl((\omega\star\omega_{1})\otimes\omega_{2}\bigr).

By linearity and continuity,
a
maps
\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}})
into itself, and
\Theta^{l}(a)
has the given form.

The second assertion is verified using a direct calculation. Indeed, if
x\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}))=\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1}))\bar{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))
then

\begin{split}\mathbbm{1}_{\operatorname{m}}\otimes\Phi(a)(x)&=\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}\bigl((((\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta^{\operatorname{op}}_{1}\otimes\textnormal{id})\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}^{*}(\mathbbm{1}_{\operatorname{m}}\otimes x)\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}})\bigr)\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}^{*}\\
&=\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}\bigl(((\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta^{\operatorname{op}}_{1}\otimes\textnormal{id}^{\otimes 3})(Z_{34}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{2,24}^{*}Z_{34}\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}\,*}_{1,13}x_{34}\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}}_{1,13}Z_{34}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{2,24}Z_{34})\bigr)\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}^{*}\\
&=\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}Z_{34}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{2,24}^{*}Z_{34}\bigl(((\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta^{\operatorname{op}}_{1}\otimes\textnormal{id}^{\otimes 3})\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}\,*}_{1,13}x_{34}\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}}_{1,13}\bigr)Z_{34}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{2,24}Z_{34}\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}^{*}\\
&=\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}Z_{34}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{2,24}^{*}Z_{34}\bigl((\omega\otimes\textnormal{id}^{\otimes 4})(\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}\,*}_{1,24}\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}\,*}_{1,14}x_{45}\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}}_{1,14}\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}}_{1,24})\bigr)Z_{34}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{2,24}Z_{34}\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}^{*}\\
&=\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}Z_{34}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{2,24}^{*}Z_{34}\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}\,*}_{1,13}\big((\omega\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}\,*}_{1,12}x_{23}\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}}_{1,12})\big)_{34}\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}}_{1,13}Z_{34}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{2,24}Z_{34}\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}^{*}\\
&=\mathbbm{1}_{\operatorname{m}}\otimes(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}\,*}_{1,12}x_{23}\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}}_{1,12}).\end{split}

Here we use that
(\Delta^{\operatorname{op}}_{1}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}}_{1}=\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}}_{1,13}\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}}_{1,23}
. The claim follows from Lemma
3.10
.
∎

Lemma 7.18
.

For
\omega\in\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G}_{2})
we have
\gamma_{2}(\lambda_{2}(\omega))\in\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}}))
. The associated maps with
b=\gamma_{2}(\lambda_{2}(\omega))
are

\Theta^{l}(b)=\operatorname{m}^{-1}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Theta^{l}(\lambda_{2}(\omega)))\operatorname{m}

and

\Phi(b)\colon\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}))\ni x\mapsto Z^{*}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Phi(\lambda_{2}(\omega)))(ZxZ^{*})Z\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}})).

Proof.

Using equation (
7.3
), we get for
\omega_{0}\in\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G}_{2})
and
\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2}\in\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G}_{1}^{\operatorname{op}})\widehat{\otimes}\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G}_{2})=\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}})
the relation

\begin{split}&\quad\;\lambda_{\operatorname{m}}(\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2})\gamma_{2}(\lambda_{2}(\omega_{0}))=\gamma_{1}(\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega_{1}))\gamma_{2}(\lambda_{2}(\omega_{2}))\gamma_{2}(\lambda_{2}(\omega_{0}))\\
&=\gamma_{1}(\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega_{1}))\gamma_{2}(\lambda_{2}(\omega_{2}\star\omega_{0}))=\lambda_{\operatorname{m}}(\omega_{1}\otimes(\omega_{2}\star\omega_{0})),\end{split}

here with
\star
the product on
\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G}_{2})
.
Thus, if
T_{0}\colon\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}})\rightarrow\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}})
is the map given by
T_{0}=\textnormal{id}\otimes(\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega_{0})\Delta_{2}
, then
T_{0}
is normal, and the pre-adjoint
(T_{0})_{*}
satisfies

\lambda_{\operatorname{m}}(\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2})\gamma_{2}(\lambda_{2}(\omega_{0}))=\lambda_{\operatorname{m}}((T_{0})_{*}(\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2})).

As
\gamma_{2}
intertwines the coproducts, it automatically intertwines the unitary antipodes (
[
50
, Proposition 3.10]
).
The same is true for
\lambda_{\operatorname{m}}
and
\lambda_{2}
, and hence we get

\begin{split}&\quad\;\lambda_{\operatorname{m}}\bigl((R_{\operatorname{m}}\circ T_{0}\circ R_{\operatorname{m}})_{*}(R_{\operatorname{m}*}(\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2}))\bigr)=\lambda_{\operatorname{m}}((T_{0}\circ R_{\operatorname{m}})_{*}(\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2}))=\widehat{R}_{\operatorname{m}}(\lambda_{\operatorname{m}}((T_{0})_{*}(\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2})))\\
&=\widehat{R}_{\operatorname{m}}\bigl(\gamma_{2}(\lambda_{2}(\omega_{0}))\bigr)\widehat{R}_{\operatorname{m}}(\lambda_{\operatorname{m}}(\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2}))=\gamma_{2}(\lambda_{2}(R_{2*}(\omega_{0})))\lambda_{\operatorname{m}}(R_{\operatorname{m}*}(\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2})).\end{split}

Now set
\omega_{0}=\omega\circ R_{2}
for our given
\omega
.
As the set of functionals of the form
R_{\operatorname{m}*}(\omega_{1}\otimes\,\omega_{2})
is linearly dense in
\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}})
, we obtain from Lemma
4.8
that
b=\gamma_{2}(\lambda_{2}(\omega))\in\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}}))
and
\Theta^{l}(b)=R_{\operatorname{m}}\circ T_{0}\circ R_{\operatorname{m}}
.
By
[
6
, Theorem 5.3]
we know that
R_{\operatorname{m}}={\operatorname{m}}^{-1}(R_{1}\otimes R_{2})=(R_{1}\otimes R_{2})\operatorname{m}
. Therefore

\displaystyle R_{\operatorname{m}}\circ T_{0}\circ R_{\operatorname{m}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{m}^{-1}(R_{1}\otimes R_{2})(\textnormal{id}\otimes(\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega\circ R_{2})\Delta_{2})(R_{1}\otimes R_{2}){\operatorname{m}}

\displaystyle={\operatorname{m}}^{-1}(\textnormal{id}\otimes R_{2}\circ(\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega\circ R_{2})\Delta_{2}\circ R_{2})\operatorname{m}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{m}^{-1}(\textnormal{id}\otimes(\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega)\Delta_{2}^{\operatorname{op}})\operatorname{m}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{m}^{-1}(\textnormal{id}\otimes(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta_{2})\operatorname{m},

and this yields the stated formula for
\Theta^{l}(b)
.

In order to verify the formula for
\Phi(b)
, recall that the unitary operator
Z
implements
\operatorname{m}
by
\operatorname{m}(\cdot)=Z\cdot Z^{*}
, and hence
\operatorname{m}^{-1}(\cdot)=Z^{*}\cdot Z
.
Moreover, from
[
6
, Proposition 3.5]
we know that
(\operatorname{m}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}})=Z_{34}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{2,24}Z_{34}\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}}_{1,13}
.
For
x\in\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}))
, by applying the expression for
\Theta^{l}(b)
just obtained, we get

\displaystyle\quad\;(\Theta^{l}(b)\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}^{*}(\mathbbm{1}_{\operatorname{m}}\otimes x)\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}})

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{m}^{-1}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\textnormal{id}\otimes(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})\Delta_{2}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\big(\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}\,*}_{1,13}Z_{34}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{2,24}^{*}Z_{34}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\otimes x)Z_{34}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{2,24}Z_{34}\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}}_{1,13}\big).

Now using
(\Delta_{2}\otimes\textnormal{id})\mathrm{W}_{2}=\mathrm{W}_{2,13}\mathrm{W}_{2,23}
, this expression becomes

\displaystyle\quad\;(\Theta^{l}(b)\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}^{*}(\mathbbm{1}_{\operatorname{m}}\otimes x)\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}})

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{m}^{-1}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})\big(\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}\,*}_{1,13}Z_{34}^{*}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\omega\otimes\textnormal{id}^{\otimes 3})(\mathrm{W}_{2,35}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{2,25}^{*}(ZxZ^{*})_{45}\mathrm{W}_{2,25}\mathrm{W}_{2,35})Z_{34}\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}}_{1,13}\big)

\displaystyle=({\operatorname{m}}^{-1}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})\big(\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}\,*}_{1,13}Z_{34}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{2,24}^{*}(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}_{2,13}^{*}(ZxZ^{*})_{23}\mathrm{W}_{2,13})_{34}\mathrm{W}_{2,24}Z_{34}\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}}_{1,13}\big).

Finally, we use the form of
\Phi(\lambda_{2}(\omega))\in\CB^{\sigma}(\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{2})))
, as in Lemma
3.10
to get

\displaystyle\quad\;(\Theta^{l}(b)\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}^{*}(\mathbbm{1}_{\operatorname{m}}\otimes x)\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}})

\displaystyle=({\operatorname{m}}^{-1}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})\big(\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}\,*}_{1,13}Z_{34}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{2,24}^{*}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Phi(\lambda_{2}(\omega)))(ZxZ^{*})_{34}\mathrm{W}_{2,24}Z_{34}\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}}_{1,13}\big).

Using again
(\operatorname{m}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}})=Z_{34}^{*}\mathrm{W}_{2,24}Z_{34}\mathrm{W}^{\operatorname{op}}_{1,13}
, we continue the calculation as

\displaystyle\quad\;(\Theta^{l}(b)\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}^{*}(\mathbbm{1}_{\operatorname{m}}\otimes x)\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}})

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{m}^{-1}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})\big((\operatorname{m}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}^{*})Z_{34}^{*}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Phi(\lambda_{2}(\omega)))(ZxZ^{*})_{34}Z_{34}(\operatorname{m}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}})\big)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}^{*}(\operatorname{m}^{-1}\otimes\textnormal{id}\otimes\textnormal{id})(Z_{34}^{*}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Phi(\lambda_{2}(\omega)))(ZxZ^{*})_{34}Z_{34})\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}^{*}Z_{12}^{*}Z_{34}^{*}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Phi(\lambda_{2}(\omega)))(ZxZ^{*})_{34}Z_{34}Z_{12}\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}},

where at the end we used that
\operatorname{m}^{-1}(\cdot)=Z^{*}\cdot Z
. It follows that

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{\operatorname{m}}\otimes\Phi(b)(x)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}(\Theta^{l}(b)\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{*}_{\operatorname{m}}(\mathbbm{1}_{\operatorname{m}}\otimes x)\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}})\mathrm{W}^{*}_{\operatorname{m}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}\big(\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}^{*}Z_{12}^{*}Z_{34}^{*}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Phi(\lambda_{2}(\omega)))(ZxZ^{*})_{34}Z_{34}Z_{12}\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}\big)\mathrm{W}_{\operatorname{m}}^{*}

\displaystyle=(Z\otimes Z)^{*}(\mathbbm{1}_{\operatorname{m}}\otimes(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Phi(\lambda_{2}(\omega)))(ZxZ^{*}))(Z\otimes Z),

and hence
\Phi(b)(x)=Z^{*}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Phi(\lambda_{2}(\omega)))(ZxZ^{*})Z
as claimed.
∎

In the next step, we shall establish continuity properties of the maps
\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}})\rightarrow\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}}))
and
\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{2}})\rightarrow\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}}))
described in Lemmas
7.17
,
7.18
. For this we need the following general fact.

Lemma 7.19
.

•

Let
E,F
be operator spaces. The map
(E\check{\otimes}F)\widehat{\otimes}F^{*}\rightarrow E
given on simple tensors by
(x\otimes y)\otimes f\mapsto\langle f,y\rangle x
is completely contractive.

•

Let
A,B
be
C^{*}
-algebras. The map
(A\otimes B)\widehat{\otimes}(A^{*}\widehat{\otimes}B^{*})\rightarrow A\widehat{\otimes}A^{*}
given on simple tensors by
(a\otimes b)\otimes(\mu\otimes\omega)\mapsto\langle\omega,b\rangle a\otimes\mu
is completely contractive.

Proof.

Due to
[
23
, Theorem 8.1.10]
the “tensor interchange” map
F^{*}\widehat{\otimes}(F\check{\otimes}E)\rightarrow(F^{*}\widehat{\otimes}F)\check{\otimes}E
, which is the formal identity on simple tensors, is a complete contraction. Since
(F^{*}\widehat{\otimes}F)^{*}\cong\CB(F^{*})
, the identity map
\textnormal{id}_{F^{*}}
induces the (completely) contractive linear
functional
F^{*}\widehat{\otimes}F\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\colon f\otimes y\mapsto\langle f,y\rangle
. Composing these complete contractions shows that
F^{*}\widehat{\otimes}(F\check{\otimes}E)\rightarrow E\colon f\otimes(y\otimes x)\mapsto\langle f,y\rangle x
is a complete contraction. By commutativity of the projective and the injective tensor products of operators spaces, respectively,
the first claim follows.

For the second part recall that the injective operator space tensor product agrees with the spatial tensor product on
C^{*}
-algebras. Using the re-bracketing isomorphism

(A\otimes B)\widehat{\otimes}(A^{*}\widehat{\otimes}B^{*})\cong(((A\otimes B)\widehat{\otimes}B^{*})\widehat{\otimes}A^{*},

the assertion hence follows by applying the first part to
E=A,F=B
and tensoring with
A^{*}
.
∎

Lemma 7.20
.

Let
(\omega_{i})_{i\in I}
be a net in
\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G}_{1}^{\operatorname{op}})
such that
\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega_{i})\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\mathbbm{1}
weak
∗
in
\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}}))
. Consider
\gamma_{1}(\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega_{i}))\in\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}}))
and
\Phi(\gamma_{1}(\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega_{i})))\in\CB^{\sigma}(\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}})))
. Then
\Phi(\gamma_{1}(\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega_{i})))

\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\textnormal{id}
weak
∗
, and thus
\gamma_{1}(\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega_{i}))\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\mathbbm{1}_{\operatorname{m}}
weak
∗
in
\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}}))
.

Proof.

By Lemma
7.17
we have
\Phi(\gamma_{1}(\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega_{i})))=\Phi(\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega_{i}))\otimes\textnormal{id}
for each
i\in I
.
Applying Lemma
7.19
with
A=\mathcal{K}(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1}))
and
B=\mathcal{K}(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))
we obtain a completely contractive map

T\colon\bigl(\mathcal{K}(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1}))\otimes\mathcal{K}(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))\bigr)\widehat{\otimes}\bigl(\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1}))_{*}\widehat{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{2}))_{*}\bigr)\rightarrow\mathcal{K}(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1}))\widehat{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{1}))_{*}

given by
T((a\otimes b)\otimes(\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2}))=\langle\omega_{2},b\rangle a\otimes\omega_{1}
on simple tensors. Recall that for any Hilbert space
\mathsf{H}
, the space of normal CB maps
\CB^{\sigma}(\B(\mathsf{H}))
has predual
\mathcal{K}(\mathsf{H})\widehat{\otimes}\B(\mathsf{H})_{*}
(see Section
3.3
). Using this identification

\begin{split}\langle\Phi(\gamma_{1}(\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega_{i}))),(a\otimes b)\otimes(\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2})\rangle&=\langle\Phi(\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega_{i}))(a),\omega_{1}\rangle\langle b,\omega_{2}\rangle\\
&=\langle\Phi(\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega_{i})),T((a\otimes b)\otimes(\omega_{1}\otimes\omega_{2}))\rangle,\end{split}

and hence
\Phi(\gamma_{1}(\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega_{i})))=T^{*}(\Phi(\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega_{i})))
. As
\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega_{i})\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\mathbbm{1}
weak
∗
, we know that
\Phi(\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega_{i}))

\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\textnormal{id}
weak
∗
, and so
\Phi(\gamma_{1}(\lambda_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}(\omega_{i})))\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}T^{*}(\textnormal{id})=\textnormal{id}
weak
∗
, as required.
∎

Lemma 7.21
.

Let
(\omega_{i})_{i\in I}
be a net in
\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G}_{2})
with
\lambda_{2}(\omega_{i})\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\mathbbm{1}
weak
∗
in
\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{2}}))
.
Then
\Phi(\gamma_{2}(\lambda_{2}(\omega_{i})))\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\textnormal{id}
weak
∗
in
\CB^{\sigma}(\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}})))
, and consequently
\gamma_{2}(\lambda_{2}(\omega_{i}))\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\mathbbm{1}_{\operatorname{m}}
weak
∗
in
\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}}))
.

Proof.

According to Lemma
7.18
we have
\Phi(\gamma_{2}(\lambda_{2}(\omega_{i})))(x)=Z^{*}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Phi(\lambda_{2}(\omega_{i})))(ZxZ^{*})Z
. We can now argue exactly as in the proof of
Lemma
7.20
. Explicitly, for
x\otimes u
in
\mathcal{K}(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}))\widehat{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}))_{*}
, the predual of
\CB^{\sigma}(\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}})))
, consider

\displaystyle\quad\;\langle\Phi(\gamma_{2}(\lambda_{2}(\omega_{i}))),x\otimes u\rangle=\langle Z^{*}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Phi(\lambda_{2}(\omega_{i})))(ZxZ^{*})Z,u\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Phi(\lambda_{2}(\omega_{i})))(ZxZ^{*}),ZuZ^{*}\rangle=\langle(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Phi(\lambda_{2}(\omega_{i}))),ZxZ^{*}\otimes ZuZ^{*}\rangle.

Since
x\mapsto ZxZ^{*}
is a complete isometry on
\mathcal{K}(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}))
and
u\mapsto ZuZ^{*}
is a complete isometry on
\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}))_{*}
, we obtain
a complete isometry
T
on
\mathcal{K}(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}))\widehat{\otimes}\B(\LL^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}))_{*}
given on simple tensors by
T(x\otimes u)=ZxZ^{*}\otimes ZuZ^{*}
. Thus

\Phi(\gamma_{2}(\lambda_{2}(\omega_{i})))=T^{*}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\Phi(\lambda_{2}(\omega_{i})))\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}T^{*}(\textnormal{id})=\textnormal{id}

weak
∗
, as required.
∎

Proof of Theorem
7.16
.

Assume that
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}}
and
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{2}}
have AP. Due to Proposition
4.3
it follows that
(\mathbb{G}_{1}^{\operatorname{op}})^{\wedge}=(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}})^{\prime}
also has AP.
Choose nets
(\omega^{(1)}_{i})_{i\in I}
in
\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G}_{1})
with
a_{i}=\lambda^{\operatorname{op}}_{1}(\omega^{(1)}_{i})\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\mathbbm{1}
weak
∗
in
\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}}))
, and similarly
(\omega^{(2)}_{j})_{j\in J}
in
\LL^{1}(\mathbb{G}_{2})
with
b_{j}=\lambda_{2}(\omega^{(2)}_{j})\xrightarrow[j\in J]{}\mathbbm{1}
weak
∗
in
\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{2}}))
. Then, by Lemmas
7.17
,
7.18
, we have
\gamma_{1}(a_{i}),\gamma_{2}(b_{j})\in\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}}))
, and (
7.3
) gives

c_{i,j}=\gamma_{1}(a_{i})\gamma_{2}(b_{j})=\lambda_{\operatorname{m}}(\omega^{(1)}_{i}\otimes\omega^{(2)}_{j})\in\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}}).

Since
\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}}))
is a dual Banach algebra, see Proposition
3.3
, it follows from Lemma
7.20
that
\lim_{i\in I}c_{i,j}=\gamma_{2}(b_{j})
weak
∗

for each
j\in J
.
Hence
\gamma_{2}(b_{j})
is contained in the weak
∗
-closure of
\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}})
in
\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}})
. Taking the limit in
j
and using Lemma
7.21

we see that
\mathbbm{1}_{\operatorname{m}}
is contained in the weak
∗
-closure of
\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}})
in
\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}}))
, as required.

The remaining statements regarding weak amenability and coamenability are verified in a similar way: if
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}},\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{2}}
are weakly amenable with
Cowling-Haagerup constants
\Lambda_{cb}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}}),\Lambda_{cb}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{2}})
then we additionally know that

\|a_{i}\|_{cb}=\|\Phi(a_{i})\|_{cb}\leq\Lambda_{cb}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}}),\quad\|b_{j}\|_{cb}=\|\Phi(b_{j})\|_{cb}\leq\Lambda_{cb}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{2}}),

and consequently
\|c_{i,j}\|_{cb}\leq\Lambda_{cb}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{1}})\,\Lambda_{cb}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{2}})
. The result then follows from Proposition
5.7
.

If
\mathbb{G}_{1},\mathbb{G}_{2}
are coamenable then we can choose
a_{i},b_{j}
such that
\sup_{i\in I,j\in J}\|c_{i,j}\|_{\A(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}})}=

\sup_{i\in I,j\in J}\|\omega_{i}\otimes\omega_{j}\|<+\infty
. The result then follows from Proposition
5.6
.
∎

7.5.
Direct products

The double crossed product construction contains as a special case the direct product of locally compact quantum groups. More precisely, assume that
\mathbb{H}_{1},\mathbb{H}_{2}
are locally compact quantum groups and let
\operatorname{m}=\textnormal{id}
be the trivial matching between
\mathbb{G}_{1}=\mathbb{H}_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}
and
\mathbb{G}_{2}=\mathbb{H}_{2}
. In this case we write
\mathbb{G}_{\operatorname{m}}=\mathbb{H}_{1}\times\mathbb{H}_{2}
and call this
the
direct product
of
\mathbb{H}_{1}
and
\mathbb{H}_{2}
. Note that this definition agrees with the usual one since

\begin{split}\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}_{1}\times\mathbb{H}_{2})&=\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{1}^{\operatorname{op}})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{2})=\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}_{1})\bar{\otimes}\LL^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}_{2}),\\
\Delta_{\mathbb{H}_{1}\times\mathbb{H}_{2}}&=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\chi\otimes\textnormal{id})(\Delta_{\mathbb{G}_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}}\otimes\Delta_{\mathbb{G}_{2}})=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\chi\otimes\textnormal{id})(\Delta_{\mathbb{H}_{1}}\otimes\Delta_{\mathbb{H}_{2}}),\end{split}

and we have
\widehat{\mathbb{H}_{1}\times\mathbb{H}_{2}}=\widehat{\mathbb{H}_{1}}\times\widehat{\mathbb{H}_{2}}
. Consequently, Corollary
7.14
and Theorem
7.16
immediately give the following result.

Proposition 7.22
.

The direct product
\mathbb{H}_{1}\times\mathbb{H}_{2}
of two locally compact quantum groups
\mathbb{H}_{1},\mathbb{H}_{2}
has AP if and only if
\mathbb{H}_{1}
and
\mathbb{H}_{2}
have AP.

8.
Categorical AP

In this section we discuss the approximation property in the setting of rigid
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-tensor categories, building
on
[
2
]
,
[
3
]
,
[
4
]
,
[
57
]
.
As an application we show in particular that the central approximation property for discrete quantum groups is invariant under monoidal equivalence.

Let us first fix some notation and terminology regarding
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-tensor categories, referring to
[
51
]
for more details and background.
If
\mathbf{T}
is a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-category and
X,Y\in\mathbf{T}
are objects we write
\mathbf{T}(X,Y)
for the space of morphisms from
X
to
Y
.
We denote by
\textnormal{id}_{X}
or
id
the identity morphism in
\mathbf{T}(X,X)
.
By definition, a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-tensor category is a
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-category
\mathbf{T}
together with a bilinear
*
-functor
\otimes:\mathbf{T}\times\mathbf{T}\rightarrow\mathbf{T}
,
a distinguished object
1\!\!1\in\mathbf{T}
and unitary natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle 1\!\!1\otimes X\cong X\cong X\otimes 1\!\!1,\qquad(X\otimes Y)\otimes Z\cong X\otimes(Y\otimes Z)

satisfying certain compatibility conditions. For simplicity we shall always assume that
\mathbf{T}
is
strict
, which means that these unitary natural isomorphisms are
identities, and we also assume that the tensor unit
1\!\!1
is simple.

A
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-tensor category
\mathbf{T}
is called
rigid
if all objects of
\mathbf{T}
are dualisable. This means that for every object
X\in\mathbf{T}
there exists an
object
X^{\vee}\in\mathbf{T}
and morphisms
s_{X}\in\mathbf{T}(X\otimes X^{\vee},1\!\!1),t_{X}\in\mathbf{T}(X^{\vee}\otimes X,1\!\!1)
which form a standard solution of the conjugate equations.
That is, we have

\displaystyle(t_{X}\otimes\textnormal{id}_{X^{\vee}})(\textnormal{id}_{X^{\vee}}\otimes s_{X}^{*})=\textnormal{id}_{X^{\vee}},\qquad(s_{X}\otimes\textnormal{id}_{X})(\textnormal{id}_{X}\otimes t_{X}^{*})=\textnormal{id}_{X},

and
s_{X}(f\otimes\textnormal{id})s_{X}^{*}=t_{X}(\textnormal{id}\otimes f)t_{X}^{*}
for all
f\in\mathbf{T}(X,X)
.
The
quantum trace

\Tr_{q}:\mathbf{T}(X,X)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
of
X
is defined by
\Tr_{q}(f)=s_{X}(f\otimes\textnormal{id})s_{X}^{*}=t_{X}(\textnormal{id}\otimes f)t_{X}^{*}
,
and the
quantum dimension
of
X
is
\dim_{q}(X)=\Tr_{q}(\textnormal{id}_{X})
.
Every rigid
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-tensor category
\mathbf{T}
is semisimple, that is, every object of
\mathbf{T}
is isomorphic to a finite direct sum of simple objects.
We write
\Irr(\mathbf{T})
for the set of isomorphism classes of simple objects in
\mathbf{T}
, and choose representatives
X_{i}\in\mathbf{T}
for elements
i=[X_{i}]\in\Irr(\mathbf{T})
,
with the convention that we also write
i=0
for the class
[1\!\!1]
.

The
fusion algebra

\mathbb{C}[\mathbf{T}]
is the vector space with basis
\Irr(\mathbf{T})
equipped with the fusion product

[X_{i}]\cdot[X_{j}]=\sum_{k\in\Irr(\mathbf{T})}N_{ij}^{k}[X_{k}],

where
N^{k}_{ij}=\dim(\mathbf{T}(X_{i}\otimes X_{j},X_{k}))
, and the
*
-structure determined by
[X_{i}]^{*}=[X_{i}^{\vee}]
. We will follow the usual abuse of notation and identify
X\in\Irr(\mathbf{T})

with its class
[X]
. The regular representation
\lambda:\mathbb{C}[\mathbf{T}]\rightarrow\B(\ell^{2}(\Irr(\mathbf{T})))
is defined by
\lambda(X)(Y)=X\cdot Y
.
The
tube algebra

\Tub(\mathbf{T})
is

(8.1)

\Tub(\mathbf{T})=\bigoplus_{i,j,k\in\Irr(\mathbf{T})}\mathbf{T}(X_{k}\otimes X_{i},X_{j}\otimes X_{k})

equipped with a suitable multiplication and
\star
-structure, see
[
27
, Definition 3.3]
.
Let us only note that
\Tub(\mathbf{T})
is a non-unital
\star
-algebra with local units, which are given by the projections
p_{i}=\textnormal{id}\in\mathbf{T}(1\!\!1\otimes X_{i},X_{i}\otimes 1\!\!1)=\mathbf{T}(X_{i},X_{i})\subseteq\Tub(\mathbf{T})
for
i\in\Irr(\mathbf{T})
.
Moreover, the corner
p_{0}\Tub(\mathbf{T})p_{0}
corresponding to
[1\!\!1]\in\Irr(\mathbf{T})
is canonically isomorphic to the fusion algebra
\mathbb{C}[\mathbf{T}]
, see
[
27
, Proposition 3.1]
.

There is a natural faithful positive trace
\Tr\colon\Tub(\mathbf{T})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
which vanishes on
\mathbf{T}(X_{k}\otimes X_{i},X_{j}\otimes X_{k})

for
i\neq j
or
X_{k}\neq 1\!\!1
, and is given by the quantum trace
\Tr_{q}
on
\mathbf{T}(1\!\!1\otimes X_{i},X_{i}\otimes 1\!\!1)
for all
i\in\Irr(\mathbf{T})
.
We write
\LL^{2}(\Tub(\mathbf{T}))
for the associated GNS-Hilbert space.
This yields the regular representation
\lambda\colon\Tub(\mathbf{T})\rightarrow\B(\LL^{2}(\Tub(\mathbf{T})))
of
\Tub(\mathbf{T})
.
The reduced
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\mathsf{red}}(\Tub(\mathbf{T}))
and the von Neumann algebra
\mathcal{L}(\Tub(\mathbf{T}))
are defined as the closure of
\lambda(\Tub(\mathbf{T}))

in the operator norm and the weak operator topology, respectively. The map
\Tr
extends to a n.s.f trace on
\mathcal{L}(\Tub(\mathbf{T}))
by
[
56
, Proposition 3.10]
.

Let us next recall the definition of multipliers on rigid
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-tensor categories from the work of Popa-Vaes
[
57
, Section 3]
.

Definition 8.1
.

Let
\mathbf{T}
be a rigid
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-tensor category.
A
multiplier
on
\mathbf{T}
is a family
\theta=(\theta_{X,Y})
of linear maps
\theta_{X,Y}:\mathbf{T}(X\otimes Y,X\otimes Y)\rightarrow\mathbf{T}(X\otimes Y,X\otimes Y)

for
X,Y\in\mathbf{T}
such that

\displaystyle\theta_{X_{2},Y_{2}}(gfh^{*})

\displaystyle=g\theta_{X_{1},Y_{1}}(f)h^{*},

\displaystyle\theta_{X_{2}\otimes X_{1},Y_{1}\otimes Y_{2}}(\textnormal{id}_{X_{2}}\otimes f\otimes\textnormal{id}_{Y_{2}})

\displaystyle=\textnormal{id}_{X_{2}}\otimes\theta_{X_{1},Y_{1}}(f)\otimes\textnormal{id}_{Y_{2}},

for all
X_{i},Y_{i}\in\mathbf{T}
,
f\in\mathbf{T}(X_{1}\otimes Y_{1},X_{1}\otimes Y_{1})
and
g,h\in\mathbf{T}(X_{1},X_{2})\otimes\mathbf{T}(Y_{1},Y_{2})\subseteq\mathbf{T}(X_{1}\otimes Y_{1},X_{2}\otimes Y_{2})
.

A multiplier
\theta=(\theta_{X,Y})
on
\mathbf{T}
is said to be completely positive (or a
CP multiplier
) if all the maps
\theta_{X,Y}
are completely positive.
A multiplier
\theta=(\theta_{X,Y})
on
\mathbf{T}
is said to be completely bounded (or a
CB multiplier
) if all the maps
\theta_{X,Y}
are completely bounded
and
\|\theta\|_{cb}=\sup_{X,Y\in\mathbf{T}}\|\theta_{X,Y}\|_{cb}<\infty
.

It is shown in
[
57
, Proposition 3.6]
that multipliers on
\mathbf{T}
are in canonical bijection with functions
\Irr(\mathbf{T})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
. We will often identify a multiplier
\theta=(\theta_{X,Y})
with its associated function
\theta=(\theta(k))_{k\in\Irr(\mathbf{T})}
. Note that we have
\|(\theta(k))_{k\in\Irr(\mathbf{T})}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\theta\|_{cb}
.

Let us write
\M_{cb}(\mathbf{T})
for the space of CB multipliers on
\mathbf{T}
. Via composition of maps and the CB norm this becomes naturally a Banach algebra. From the definition of the correspondence between functions on
\Irr(\mathbf{T})
and multipliers, compare
[
57
, Formula (3.5)]
, it follows that the product on
\M_{cb}(\mathbf{T})
corresponds to pointwise multiplication of functions. It is shown in
[
2
, Corollary 5.3]
that
\M_{cb}(\mathbf{T})
is a dual Banach algebra, whose predual
Q(\mathbf{T})
can be constructed using
the tube algebra of
\mathbf{T}
. More specifically, if
\theta\in\M_{cb}(\mathbf{T})
is a CB multiplier, define
M_{\theta}\colon\Tub(\mathbf{T})\rightarrow\Tub(\mathbf{T})
by

M_{\theta}(f)=\theta(k)f,\qquad f\in\mathbf{T}(X_{k}\otimes X_{i},X_{j}\otimes X_{k})\subseteq\Tub(\mathbf{T}).

Due to
[
4
, Proposition 5.1]
the map
M
gives an isometric embedding of
\M_{cb}(\mathbf{T})
into the space
\CB^{\sigma}(\mathcal{L}(\Tub(\mathbf{T})))

of normal CB maps on
\mathcal{L}(\Tub(\mathbf{T}))
,
and also an isometric embedding with weak
∗
-closed image
\M_{cb}(\mathbf{T})\rightarrow\CB(\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\mathsf{red}}(\Tub(\mathbf{T})),\mathcal{L}(\Tub(\mathbf{T})))
.
Then the predual
Q(\mathbf{T})
of
\M_{cb}(\mathbf{T})
obtained in
[
2
]
is constructed as the resulting quotient of the
predual
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\mathsf{red}}(\Tub(\mathbf{T}))\widehat{\otimes}\mathcal{L}(\Tub(\mathbf{T}))_{*}
of
\CB(\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\mathsf{red}}(\Tub(\mathbf{T})),\mathcal{L}(\Tub(\mathbf{T})))
– compare Theorem
3.8
.

We can approximate elements of
Q(\mathbf{T})
by taking tensor products of elements in
\Tub(\mathbf{T})
and vector functionals associated to vectors from
\LL^{2}(\Tub(\mathbf{T}))
.
Noting that
\Tub(\mathbf{T})
is dense in
\LL^{2}(\Tub(\mathbf{T}))
, a linearly dense collection of functionals in
\mathcal{L}(\Tub(\mathbf{T}))_{*}
is given by
\mathcal{L}(\Tub(\mathbf{T}))\ni T\mapsto\langle f\,|\,Tg\rangle=\Tr(f^{*}Tg)=\Tr(Tgf^{*})
for
f,g\in\Tub(\mathbf{T})
. As
\Tub(\mathbf{T})
has local units, we have
\{gf^{*}\,|\,f,g\in\Tub(\mathbf{T})\}=\Tub(\mathbf{T})
and it suffices to look at functionals of the form
T\mapsto\Tr(Tf)
for
f\in\Tub(\mathbf{T})
. Under this identification, the canonical pairing of
f\otimes g\in\Tub(\mathbf{T})\odot\Tub(\mathbf{T})\subseteq\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\mathsf{red}}(\Tub(\mathbf{T}))\widehat{\otimes}\mathcal{L}(\Tub(\mathbf{T}))_{*}
with
\theta\in\M_{cb}(\mathbf{T})
becomes

(8.2)

\langle\theta,f\otimes g\rangle=\Tr(gM_{\theta}(f)).

Let us define a weighted
\ell^{1}
-norm on
\mathrm{c}_{00}(\Irr(\mathbf{T}))
by

\|f\|_{1}=\sum_{k\in\Irr(\mathbf{T})}\dim_{q}(X_{k})|f(k)|,

and denote by
\ell^{1}(\Irr(\mathbf{T}))
the corresponding completion, compare
[
57
, Remark 10.4]
.
The weighting by quantum dimensions ensures that admissible
*
-representations of
\mathbb{C}[\mathbf{T}]
in the sense of
[
57
, Definition 4.1]

extend to contractive
*
-representations of
\ell^{1}(\Irr(\mathbf{T}))
.
Note that there is a contractive embedding
\iota\colon\ell^{1}(\Irr(\mathbf{T}))\rightarrow\M_{cb}(\mathbf{T})^{*}
given by

\iota(\omega)(\theta)=\sum_{k\in\Irr(\mathbf{T})}\dim_{q}(X_{k})\omega(k)\theta(k)\qquad(\omega\in\ell^{1}(\Irr(\mathbf{T})),\theta\in\M_{cb}(\mathbf{T})).

Lemma 8.2
.

Let
\mathbf{T}
be a rigid
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-tensor category. Then the Banach space
Q(\mathbf{T})
can be identified with the closure of
\ell^{1}(\Irr(\mathbf{T}))
in
\M_{cb}(\mathbf{T})^{*}

under the embedding
\iota
.

Proof.

It follows from the explicit formulas that the image of the subspace
\Tub(\mathbf{T})\odot\Tub(\mathbf{T})\subseteq\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\mathsf{red}}(\Tub(\mathbf{T}))\widehat{\otimes}\mathcal{L}(\Tub(\mathbf{T}))_{*}

in
\M_{cb}(\mathbf{T})^{*}
agrees with the image of
\mathrm{c}_{00}(\Irr(\mathbf{T}))
under the map
\iota
.
Indeed, that we obtain all of
\mathrm{c}_{00}(\Irr(\mathbf{T}))
in this way can be seen by considering
f,g\in p_{0}\Tub(\mathbf{T})p_{0}\cong\mathbb{C}[\mathbf{T}]
in (
8.2
).
The claim therefore follows from density of the former space inside
Q(\mathbf{T})
.
∎

Remembering that the weak
∗
-topology on
\M_{cb}(\mathbf{T})
means the one induced by the predual
Q(\mathbf{T})
, we shall now give the following definition.

Definition 8.3
.

Let
\mathbf{T}
be a rigid
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-tensor category. We say that
\mathbf{T}
has the
approximation property (AP)
if there exists a net of finitely supported CB multipliers of
\mathbf{T}

converging to
\mathbbm{1}
in the weak
∗
-topology of
\M_{cb}(\mathbf{T})
.

Comparing with
[
57
, Definition 5.1]
we see that every weakly amenable rigid
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-tensor category has AP.
Indeed, a uniformly bounded net of finitely supported CB multipliers converging pointwise to
\mathbbm{1}

converges also in the weak
∗
-topology since
\mathrm{c}_{00}(\Irr(\mathbf{T}))
is dense in
Q(\mathbf{T})
by Lemma
8.2
.

Next recall the notion of central approximation property for discrete quantum groups from Definition
6.3
.
We aim to show that the central approximation property for a discrete quantum group
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
is equivalent to the approximation property for the rigid
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-tensor
category
\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
of finite dimensional unitary corepresentations of
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
(i.e. representations of
\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}
). To make the notation more coherent, we will write in this section
\mathbb{C}[\mathbbold{\Gamma}]=\Pol(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
and
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\mathsf{red}}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})=\mathrm{C}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
,
\mathcal{L}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})=\LL^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
, and use the same conventions for the Drinfeld double
D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
.

We shall first discuss the relation between categorical AP for
\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
and AP for the Drinfeld double
D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
of
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
.
Recall from Section
7.4.1
that
\LL^{\infty}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))=\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{L}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
with the coproduct

\Delta_{D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\chi\otimes\textnormal{id})(\textnormal{id}\otimes\ad(\mathrm{W})\otimes\textnormal{id})(\Delta\otimes\widehat{\Delta}),

where
\mathrm{W}\in\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{L}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
is the Kac-Takesaki operator of
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
.
Note also that we have a canonical identification of the center
\mathcal{Z}\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
of
\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})

with
\ell^{\infty}(\Irr(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})))=\ell^{\infty}(\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}))
. In particular,
every multiplier
\theta\in\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
can be viewed as a (central) element of
\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
.

In what follows we will use the notion of an
algebraic quantum group
, see
[
69
]
,
[
72
, Section 3.2]
. By definition, an algebraic quantum group is described via a multiplier Hopf
\star
-algebra for which there exists a positive left invariant functional and a positive right invariant functional. For example, if
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
is a discrete quantum group then
\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})

and
\mathbb{C}[\mathbbold{\Gamma}]
equipped with their respective comultiplications and Haar integrals are examples of algebraic quantum groups. Every algebraic quantum group gives rise to a locally compact quantum group in the sense of Kustermans-Vaes via an appropriate completion procedure, see
[
45
]
. Moreover, one finds that all elements of the underlying
multiplier Hopf
\star
-algebra are contained in the Fourier algebra of the locally compact quantum group, see e.g. the end of Section
1
in
[
45
]
.

The Drinfeld double
D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
of a discrete quantum group
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
and its dual
\widehat{D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})}
are also algebraic quantum
groups. The corresponding multiplier Hopf
*
-algebras are
\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})\odot\mathbb{C}[\mathbbold{\Gamma}]\subseteq\LL^{\infty}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
and

\mathcal{D}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))=\mathbb{C}[\mathbbold{\Gamma}]\bowtie\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})=\lin\{\gamma_{1}(\widehat{x})\gamma_{2}(x)\,|\,\widehat{x}\in\mathbb{C}[\mathbbold{\Gamma}],x\in\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})\}\subseteq\mathcal{L}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})),

where

\begin{split}\gamma_{1}&\colon\mathcal{L}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))\colon\widehat{x}\mapsto\widehat{x}\otimes\mathbbm{1},\\
\gamma_{2}&\colon\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))\colon x\mapsto Z^{*}(\mathbbm{1}\otimes x)Z\end{split}

are the maps introduced in Lemma
7.13
. We will write
\gamma_{1}(\widehat{x})\gamma_{2}(x)=\widehat{x}\bowtie x
for
\widehat{x}\in\mathbb{C}[\mathbbold{\Gamma}],x\in\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
.

Lemma 8.4
.

Let
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
be a discrete quantum group and let
D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
be its Drinfeld double. There is an isometric embedding

N\colon\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))\rightarrow\CB^{\sigma}(\mathcal{L}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})))\colon\theta\mapsto N_{\theta}

given by

N_{\theta}(U^{\alpha}_{i,j}\bowtie x_{\beta})=\theta(\alpha)U^{\alpha}_{i,j}\bowtie x_{\beta}

for
U^{\alpha}_{i,j}\in\mathbb{C}[\mathbbold{\Gamma}],x_{\beta}\in\B(\mathsf{H}_{\beta})\subseteq\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
.
If
\theta\in\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
then
\theta\otimes\mathbbm{1}\in\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})))

\subseteq\LL^{\infty}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))

and
N_{\theta}=\Theta^{l}(\theta\otimes\mathbbm{1})
.

Proof.

We wish to apply the results of
[
52
, Section 3]
. For this we need to work with the annular algebra

\Tub(\mathbbold{\Gamma})=\bigoplus_{\alpha,\beta\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})}\Big(\bigoplus_{\gamma\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})}\Mor(\gamma\otimes\alpha,\beta\otimes\gamma)\Big)\otimes\B(\mathsf{H}_{\overline{\alpha}},\mathsf{H}_{\overline{\beta}}),

which is equipped with the multiplication given by the product from
\Tub(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
in (
8.1
) and the composition of operators between
the Hilbert spaces
\mathsf{H}_{\gamma}
.
We refer to
[
27
, Section 3]
for the general definition of annular algebras associated with full weight sets.

We again obtain a trace on
\Tub(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
and so can perform the GNS construction, and construct the associated von Neumann algebra
\mathcal{L}(\Tub(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
.
Furthermore,
[
4
, Proposition 5.1]
applies, and we obtain a map
\widetilde{M}\colon\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))\rightarrow\CB^{\sigma}(\mathcal{L}(\Tub(\mathbbold{\Gamma})))

given by
\widetilde{M}_{\theta}(f)=\theta(\gamma)f

for
f=f^{\prime}\otimes T\in\Mor(\gamma\otimes\alpha,\beta\otimes\gamma)\otimes\B(\mathsf{H}_{\overline{\alpha}},\mathsf{H}_{\overline{\beta}})\subseteq\Tub(\mathbbold{\Gamma})\subseteq\mathcal{L}(\Tub(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
, which is a well-defined isometric embedding.

It is shown in
[
52
, Theorem 3.5]
that there is a
\star
-isomorphism between
\Tub(\mathbbold{\Gamma})

and the algebraic convolution algebra
\mathcal{D}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))=\mathbb{C}[\mathbbold{\Gamma}]\bowtie\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
of the Drinfeld double of
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
.
Under this isomorphism, the trace
\Tr
on
\Tub(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
does not correspond to the left invariant functional on
\mathcal{D}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
on the nose,
but both functionals can be obtained from one another by multiplication with a positive invertible element in the algebraic multiplier algebra of
\Tub(\mathbbold{\Gamma})\cong\mathcal{D}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
.
It follows that the regular representations of
\Tub(\mathbbold{\Gamma})\cong\mathcal{D}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})))
on
\LL^{2}(\Tub(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
and
\LL^{2}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
are unitarily equivalent, which means that the
isomorphism in
[
52
, Theorem 3.5]
induces a normal
\star
-isomorphism
\mathcal{L}(\Tub(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))\cong\mathcal{L}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
,
which restricts to a
\star
-isomorphism
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\mathsf{red}}(\Tub(\mathbf{T}))\cong\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\mathsf{red}}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
.

Inspecting the formulas in
[
52
]
one checks that
\widetilde{M}_{\theta}\colon\Tub(\mathbbold{\Gamma})\rightarrow\Tub(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
identifies under
the isomorphism
\Tub(\mathbbold{\Gamma})\cong\mathcal{D}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})))
with the map
N_{\theta}\colon\mathcal{D}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
in the statement of the lemma.
Consequently, we see that
N_{\theta}
extends to a normal CB map on
\mathcal{L}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
.

An explicit formula for the multiplication in
\mathcal{D}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
is given in
[
72
, page 219]
, though be aware that
[
72
]
uses a different convention to us, with the factors
\mathbb{C}[\mathbbold{\Gamma}]
and
\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
swapped around; in what follows, we make the necessarily changes to follow our conventions. It follows that, for
x\in\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
, we have that
\gamma_{2}(x)\gamma_{1}(U^{\alpha}_{i,j})\in\lin\{\gamma_{1}(U^{\alpha}_{k,l})\gamma_{2}(y)\,|\,1\leq k,l\leq\dim(\alpha),y\in\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})\}
and so
N_{\theta}(\gamma_{2}(x)\gamma_{1}(U^{\alpha}_{i,j}))=\theta(\alpha)\gamma_{2}(x)\gamma_{1}(U^{\alpha}_{i,j})
.

From Section
7.4.1
, we find that

\mathrm{W}^{D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})*}=Z_{34}^{*}\widehat{\mathrm{W}}_{24}^{*}Z_{34}\mathrm{W}_{13}^{*}=(\textnormal{id}\otimes\gamma_{2})(\widehat{\mathrm{W}}^{*})_{234}(\textnormal{id}\otimes\gamma_{1})(\mathrm{W}^{*})_{134},

where
\mathrm{W}=\mathrm{W}{\Gamma}
. Given
\alpha,i,j
, there is
\omega\in\ell^{1}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
with
(\omega\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{*})=U^{\alpha}_{i,j}
, because
\mathrm{W}^{*}=\chi(\widehat{\mathrm{W}})
and
\widehat{\mathrm{W}}
is given by (
6.2
). Then for
\widehat{\omega}\in\LL^{1}(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})
,

\displaystyle(\omega\otimes\widehat{\omega}\otimes N_{\theta})(\mathrm{W}^{D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})*})

\displaystyle=N_{\theta}\big(\gamma_{2}((\widehat{\omega}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathrm{W}}^{*}))\gamma_{1}(U^{\alpha}_{i,j}))\big)=\theta(\alpha)\gamma_{2}((\widehat{\omega}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\widehat{\mathrm{W}}^{*}))\gamma_{1}(U^{\alpha}_{i,j}))

using the previous observation. Given
x_{\beta}\in\B(\mathsf{H}_{\beta})\subseteq\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
, notice that
\theta x_{\beta}=\theta(\beta)x_{\beta}
, and so
\omega\theta=\theta(\alpha)\omega
, as
\omega\in\B(\mathsf{H}_{\alpha})^{*}
. Thus

(\omega\otimes\widehat{\omega}\otimes N_{\theta})(\mathrm{W}^{D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})*})=\theta(\alpha)(\omega\otimes\widehat{\omega}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})*})=(\omega\otimes\widehat{\omega}\otimes\textnormal{id})(((\theta\otimes\mathbbm{1})\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathrm{W}^{D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})*}).

As such
\omega
are linearly dense, it follows that
(\textnormal{id}\otimes N_{\theta})(\mathrm{W}^{D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})*})=((\theta\otimes\mathbbm{1})\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathrm{W}^{D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})*}
or equivalently,
(N_{\theta}\otimes\textnormal{id})(\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})}})=(\mathbbm{1}\otimes(\theta\otimes\mathbbm{1}))\mathrm{W}^{\widehat{D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})}}
.

We conclude that
\theta\otimes\mathbbm{1}\in\LL^{\infty}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))=\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})\bar{\otimes}\mathcal{L}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
satisfies
\theta\otimes\mathbbm{1}\in\M^{l}_{cb}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))

and
\Theta^{l}(\theta\otimes\mathbbm{1})=N_{\theta}
as claimed.
∎

Similar to Proposition
3.6
, we can restrict
N_{\theta}
to a map in
\CB\bigl(\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\mathsf{red}}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})),\mathcal{L}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))\bigr)
. As
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\mathsf{red}}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
is weak
∗
-dense in
\mathcal{L}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
, Kaplansky density shows that this restriction map is a (complete) isometry.

Lemma 8.5
.

Let
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
be a discrete quantum group. The map

N\colon\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))\rightarrow\CB\bigl(\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\mathsf{red}}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})),\mathcal{L}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))\bigr)

of Lemma
8.4
is weak
∗
-weak
∗
-continuous.

Proof.

As in the proof of Lemma
8.4
, we identify
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\mathsf{red}}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
with
\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\mathsf{red}}(\Tub(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
and
\mathcal{L}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
with
\mathcal{L}(\Tub(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
.
Then
N
identifies with
\widetilde{M}:\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))\rightarrow\CB(\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\mathsf{red}}(\Tub(\mathbbold{\Gamma})),\mathcal{L}(\Tub(\mathbbold{\Gamma})))
,
and we note that
\widetilde{M}
is again isometric.

It hence suffices to show that
\widetilde{M}
is weak
∗
-weak
∗
-continuous, for which we shall apply Lemma
3.7
with
\alpha=\widetilde{M},E=Q(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))

and
F=\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\mathsf{red}}(\Tub(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))\widehat{\otimes}\mathcal{L}(\Tub(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))_{*}
. From the definition of
\Tub(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
in the proof of Lemma
8.4
,
we see that the elements of the form
\omega=(f\otimes T)\otimes\Tr(\cdot(g\otimes S))
with

f\otimes T\in\Mor(\gamma\otimes\alpha,\beta\otimes\gamma)\otimes\B(\mathsf{H}_{\overline{\alpha}},\mathsf{H}_{\overline{\beta}})\subseteq\Tub(\mathbbold{\Gamma})

and
g\otimes S\in\Mor(\gamma^{\prime}\otimes\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}\otimes\gamma^{\prime})\otimes\B(\mathsf{H}_{\overline{\alpha^{\prime}}},\mathsf{H}_{\overline{\beta^{\prime}}})
form a linearly dense subset
D\subseteq F
.

For such an
\omega
, given
\theta\in\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
we calculate that

\langle\widetilde{M}_{\theta},\omega\rangle=\langle\widetilde{M}_{\theta},(f\otimes T)\otimes\Tr(\cdot(g\otimes S))\rangle=\Tr\big(\widetilde{M}_{\theta}(f\otimes T)(g\otimes S)\big)=\theta(\gamma)\Tr(fg\otimes TS).

Hence the function
\theta\mapsto\langle\widetilde{M}_{\theta},\omega\rangle
lies in the image of
\mathrm{c}_{00}(\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}))
inside
\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))^{*}
–
in particular in
Q(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
by Lemma
8.2
. This verifies the condition of Lemma
3.7
, and the claim follows.
∎

Write
\mathcal{Z}\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
for the center of the Banach algebra
\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
and note that
\mathcal{Z}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))=\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))\cap\mathcal{Z}\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
. Furthermore, observe that
\mathcal{Z}\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))\subseteq\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
is weak
∗
-closed, hence
\mathcal{Z}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
is a dual space, with distinguished predual being a quotient of
Q^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
.

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
be a discrete quantum group. Then there is a canonical isometric isomorphism
\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))\cong\mathcal{Z}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
,
and this isomorphism is a weak
∗
-weak
∗
-homeomorphism.

Proof.

Let
\theta\colon\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
be a bounded function, identified with a central element of
\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
. We shall first verify that
\theta
is contained in
\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
if and only if it is contained in
\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
, and that the corresponding CB norms agree.

Firstly, let
\theta\in\mathcal{Z}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
, considered as a map
\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
. In
[
57
, Section 6]
, the associated centraliser is denoted by
\Psi_{\theta}
, see
[
57
, Equation (6.1)]
, and then
[
57
, Proposition 6.1]
shows that if
\Psi_{\theta}
is completely bounded, which under our assumption it is, then the multiplier on
\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
given by
\theta
is also completely bounded, with
\|\theta\|_{\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))}\leq\|\theta\|_{\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))}
.

Conversely, when
\theta\in\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
, then by Lemma
8.4
, we know that
\theta\otimes\mathbbm{1}\in\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})))
. We again use the normal injective
\star
-homomorphism
\gamma_{1}:\mathcal{L}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
which identifies
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
as a closed quantum subgroup of
D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
, see Lemma
7.13
.
As
\gamma_{1}(\widehat{x})=\widehat{x}\otimes\mathbbm{1}
, it follows from Lemma
8.4
that
\Theta^{l}(\theta\otimes\mathbbm{1})=N_{\theta}
leaves the image of
\gamma_{1}
invariant, and so we obtain a map
L_{\theta}\in\CB^{\sigma}(\mathcal{L}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
with
\gamma_{1}L_{\theta}=N_{\theta}\gamma_{1}
and
\|L_{\theta}\|_{cb}\leq\|N_{\theta}\|_{cb}=\|\theta\|_{cb}
. In particular,

L_{\theta}(U^{\alpha}_{i,j})=\theta(\alpha)U^{\alpha}_{i,j}
for each
\alpha,i,j
.
Thus
\theta\in\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
, with
\|\theta\|_{\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))}\geq\|\theta\|_{\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))}
.

As these identifications are mutual inverses, we have shown that
\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))\cong\mathcal{Z}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
isometrically. Let
\gamma:\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
be the resulting isometric isomorphism, which we claim is weak
∗
-weak
∗
-continuous. We again use Lemma
3.7
, with
E=Q(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
and
F
the predual of
\mathcal{Z}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
, with
D\subseteq F
to be constructed. As the predual of
\mathcal{Z}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
is a quotient of
Q^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
, it suffices to take
D
to be the image under the quotient map of a linearly dense subset
D^{\prime}
of
Q^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
. We take
D^{\prime}\subseteq\ell^{1}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})\subseteq Q^{l}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
to consist of all linear functionals
\omega
constructed by choosing
x\in\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
and defining

\langle y,\omega\rangle=\sum_{\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})}\dim_{q}(\alpha)\Tr_{\alpha}(y_{\alpha}x_{\alpha})
for
y\in\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
. Given
\theta\in\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
and
\omega\in D
induced by
x\in\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
, we see that

\langle\gamma(\theta),\omega\rangle=\sum_{\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})}\dim_{q}(\alpha)\theta(\alpha)\Tr_{\alpha}(x_{\alpha}),

where the sum is finite. Hence if we set
z=(\Tr_{\alpha}(x_{\alpha}))_{\alpha\in\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma})}\in\mathrm{c}_{00}(\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}))

then
\langle\gamma(\theta),\omega\rangle=\langle\theta,\iota(z)\rangle
, where
\iota
is the embedding of
\ell^{1}(\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}))
into
Q(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
as in Lemma
8.2
. It follows that
\omega\circ\gamma\in Q(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
, and hence
\gamma^{*}\kappa_{F}(D)\subseteq\kappa_{E}(E)
. Now Lemma
3.7
yields the claim.
∎

Let us now compare the categorical approximation property of
\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
with the central approximation property of
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
.

Proposition 8.7
.

A discrete quantum group
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
has central AP if and only if
\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
has AP.

Proof.

The claim follows from Lemma
8.6
, as the isomorphism
\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))\simeq\mathcal{Z}\M^{l}_{cb}(\A(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
is a unital weak
∗
-weak
∗
-homeomorphism which restricts
to
\mathrm{c}_{00}(\Irr(\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}))\simeq\mathcal{Z}\mathrm{c}_{00}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
.
∎

As a consequence of Proposition
8.7
we see that central AP is invariant under monoidal equivalence.

Corollary 8.8
.

Let
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
and
\mathbbold{\Lambda}
be discrete quantum groups such that
\widehat{\mathbbold}{\Gamma}
and
\widehat{\mathbbold{\Lambda}}
are monoidally equivalent. Then
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
has central AP if and only if
\mathbbold{\Lambda}
has central AP.

Proof.

According to the definition of monoidal equivalence
[
8
]
, the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-tensor categories
\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
and
\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Lambda})
are unitarily monoidally equivalent.
This means that
\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
has AP if and only if
\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Lambda})
has AP. Due to Proposition
8.7
this yields the claim.
∎

Finally, let us relate AP of
\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
and
D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
.

Proposition 8.9
.

Let
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
be a discrete quantum group such that
\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
has AP. Then the Drinfeld double
D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
has AP. If
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
is unimodular, then the converse also holds: AP of
D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
implies AP of
\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
.

Proof.

Due to Lemma
8.4
we have an isometric embedding
\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))\rightarrow\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})))
given by
\theta\mapsto\theta\otimes\mathbbm{1}
. As
\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
has AP, there is a net
(\theta_{i})_{i\in I}
of finitely supported elements in
\M_{cb}(\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
with
\theta_{i}\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\mathbbm{1}
in the weak
∗
-topology. By Lemma
8.5
, it follows that the net
(N_{\theta_{i}})_{i\in I}
converges weak
∗
to the inclusion map in
\CB\bigl(\mathrm{C}^{*}_{\mathsf{red}}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})),\mathcal{L}(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))\bigr)
. As
N_{\theta_{i}}=\Theta^{l}(\theta_{i}\otimes\mathbbm{1})
for each
i
, by Theorem
3.8
this means that
\theta_{i}\otimes\mathbbm{1}\xrightarrow[i\in I]{}\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}
weak
∗
in
\M_{cb}^{l}(\A(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})))
. Since the elements
\theta_{i}\otimes\mathbbm{1}
belong to the multiplier Hopf
\star
-algebra, they also belong to the Fourier algebra
\A(D(\mathbbold{\Gamma}))
and we conclude that
D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
has AP.

If
D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
has AP then by Theorem
7.1
the same is true for
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
since
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
is a closed quantum subgroup of
D(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
. If
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
is in addition unimodular,
then AP of
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
implies central AP of
\mathbbold{\Gamma}
by Proposition
6.8
, and consequently AP of
\mathsf{Corep}(\mathbbold{\Gamma})
due to Proposition
8.7
.
∎
